Before each click or key `tools/game-input.ps1` sent, the foreground window was checked to be the game's; it was each time.

- **Startup**: 580 dependency points match; 106 hooks, 4 writes. The settings line has the three faces, `heading_font: Face { family: "Microsoft YaHei", weight: 700 }` among them.
- **The pages**: `standard_font`, `ToolTip_Font` and `Arial12` (Body) in "Microsoft YaHei" 400, `standard_22` and `Main_24` (Heading) in "Microsoft YaHei" 700, `tahoma_60` (Map) in the map font at 400. No weight stepped down, no font drew from a page in another face, no page was left as it was.
- **The settings window**, opened from the main menu's corner button: its eight lists, 文字外观, 正文字体 微软雅黑, 正文字重 Regular, 标题字体 微软雅黑, 标题字重 Bold, 地图字体 方正盛世楷书简体_大, 地图字重 Regular, 地图文字包边, and the restart line and the two buttons, fit in a window about half the screen tall at 3840x2160. 标题字重 opened on Light, Regular and Bold, Bold marked. Escape closed it unsaved; the framework's `settings.txt` was as written before the run.
- **A game**: `menu.start MNG`, never unpaused. In the start-of-game introduction the title, the tabs, 大明, the description and 不再显示 are in Microsoft YaHei Bold; the Close button and the outliner in Regular. The description's first line holds more characters than with Cu YaSong in the run above; why was not looked into.
- **Not seen in the running game**: a weight chosen in the settings window and saved (the heading list was only opened; the weight lists' behavior when the family changes was tried by the player on master, for body text and the map); the framework UI in another body text weight than 400; bitmap fonts with this build.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process; no save was written and no crash folder made. `tools/player-state.ps1 -Restore` listed what the run changed (the framework's logs and settings, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`), put each back and found every file as the backup had it. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies. The backups and screenshots of the run were deleted.

## 2026-10-31 Army orders that change nothing (tested by me)

The run of 2026-10-30 saw `army_ordered` for the Ottomans' army after a right click on the Sea of Marmara, its route unchanged. Two runs of `eu4.exe`, started directly, each a new game as Castile at 1444.11.11 (`menu.start CAS`), paused but for days passed with `time.advance`. Backups as AGENTS.md says, with the framework files installed in the game folder and the mod's DLL copied aside; the regional-combat extension was rebuilt from its repository's master against this SDK, in a copy of the repository, and its DLL replaced the mod's for the runs. An army was selected by clicking its model after `camera.look_at_province`, and ordered with a right click (`tools/game-input.ps1`). What the game does is in [research/army-orders.md](research/army-orders.md).

- **First run, master with logging added for the run** (each movement command posted, each move and stop of the game's for an own army with its route before and after, each order heard at a safe point):
  - The army in Toledo, four days on its way to La Mancha (`2754`) and movement-locked: a right click on Albacete was posted, heard at the next safe point, and carried out 9 ms after the post, two installs making the route `[2754, 4547]`; one on Toledo gave `[2754, 219]`, and the same again `[2754, 219]`, set anew. Each was heard two or three times. Right clicks on the Gulf of Valencia and on Mallorca posted nothing.
  - The army in Asturias, with no route: a right click on the Cantabrian Sea next to it, with no fleet there, was posted and heard as `army_ordered`, and no install followed; the route stayed empty. So too with the army movement-locked on its way to `4554` with `206` next: heard, and the route `[4554, 206]` as it was. That is what the run of 2026-10-30 saw.
- **Second run, this change** (576 dependency points match; 105 hooks, 4 writes; the extension loaded), the army in Asturias:
  - With regional combat's task turned on by its button ("task on: center 207 radius 5"), the right click on the Cantabrian Sea raised no event, the route stayed empty and the task on. A right click on León raised one `army_ordered`, made the route `[208]` and turned the task off ("task off: manual order").
  - A right click on Asturias itself, with that route: one event, the route cleared; again, with none: no event.
  - `army.move` to `206`: one event. Five days later, movement-locked on the way to `4554` and the task on again: the Cantabrian Sea, no event and the task still on; León, two events, the route `[4554, 208]` and the task off; León again, two events and the same provinces.

- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process; no crash folder was made. `player-state.ps1 -Restore` listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`), put each back and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files and the mod's DLL were put back and compared equal to their copies; the regional-combat repository has no change. ## 2026-11-01 Light text drawn as a blend in light would show it (tested by me)

The player found body text in FZ PingXian YaSong far from how the Chinese language mod draws it in the same face. System glyphs for light text are now drawn with the alpha that gives over black what a blend in light would, and fonts drawing dark text from the same page get glyphs of their own, with their coverage as it was. Three runs of `eu4.exe`, started directly, Chinese playset with the unchanged regional-combat extension, at 3840x2160 with the interface scale at 1.5, the player's framework settings: body text in FZPingXianYaSongS-R-GB 400, headings in FZYaSongS-B-GB 400, the map in FZShengShiKaiShuS-EB-GB 400. The first run was master at `0dd80ca` (577 dependency points match), with system fonts; the second this branch's build, after the build, the tests, clippy and the offline checks against `eu4.exe` passed, with system fonts; the third the same build with bitmap fonts, to see the mod's own pages. Each went to the main menu, then `menu.start MNG`, `console run event flavor_mng.1`, a click on the start-of-game introduction's Close button (the game window checked to be in front first), and screenshots through `eu4-harmony game screenshot`. - **Startup**: 579 dependency points match, the font color's two among them; 105 hooks, 4 writes.

- **Light text**: the tooltip (`ToolTip_Font`), the event's options (`vic_18`), its title (`vic_29s`) and the introduction's description (`vic_22`) are brighter, their edges fuller; the tooltip's text changed in 19,801 of 73,440 pixels, the most by 169 of 255. The headings in FZ Cu YaSong, mostly whole pixels already, changed less to the eye.

## 2026-11-02 Extension save data in the save (tested by me)

Extension save data now goes into the save itself (ADR extension-save-data-in-the-save). One run of `eu4.exe`, started directly and started again by `menu.return` between loads, Chinese playset with the unchanged regional-combat extension, at 3840x2160. This branch's build with the `trace` feature, tracing the game's checksum check `0x97500` and later the checksum `0x14e3390`, after the build, the tests, clippy and the offline checks against `eu4.exe` passed. Backups as AGENTS.md says, with the framework files installed in the game folder copied aside first. The game was driven by `eu4-harmony game`; test saves were made with a scratch script that writes the entry as the framework does and redoes the checksum with `I_am_such_a_cheater` (research/saves.md), after it matched the checksums of the player's saves salted so.

- **Startup**: 581 dependency points match; 103 hooks, 4 writes; the extension loaded. After the change below, 582 points.
- **No data, nothing written**: a new game as Castile (`menu.start CAS`) ran to the half-year autosave of 1445.1.1, a plain text save (`compress_autosave=no`), with no extension data: no line from `saves`, and no `eu4_harmony` in the file.
- **Two clicks went astray**: the start-of-game introduction was to be closed with two clicks from `tools/game-input.ps1`; it stayed open, and the foreground window was then the player's Chrome, so the clicks may have landed there. No further input was sent, and the regional-combat task was never turned on by its button; the data came from the test saves instead.
- **An entry after the checksum is never read**: a copy of that autosave with the entry appended after the checksum loaded with "data for 0 extension(s)". The text tokenizer logged "Quoted string doesn't close" once for each of the entry's five strings, and the game's check failed, the checksum's span having moved.
- **A defect, fixed in the run**: the entry put before the checksum, after the AI as the framework wrote it then (the world write's `after` observer), loaded with "data for 0 extension(s)" and no line in `error.log`. In a plain save the game reads top-level entries only up to the AI (research/saves.md). The entry is now written where the world has written its game state, before the AI (a jump at `0x751c87`).
- **Plain text**: the entry before the AI, with regional-combat's task for the army in Asturias (`850403524662`, center 207, radius 5) and 1,400 bytes for an extension that is not loaded (`rh-filler`), in five strings of up to 500 bytes. Loading it: "data for 2 extension(s)", "dropped the data of "rh-filler", which is not loaded", and the extension "restored 1 task(s)". The autosave of 1445.7.1 then wrote "the data of 1 extension(s), 1 string(s)": in the file, the entry between `idea_dates` and `ai`, in the layout ADR extension-save-data-in-the-save gives, and the file's checksum, with `I_am_such_a_cheater`, took it in. Loaded again: "data for 1 extension(s)", "restored 1 task(s)".
- **Zipped text** (`compress_autosave=yes` for the rest of the run): from that autosave to the one of 1446.1.1, `gamestate` held the entry last before the checksum, which took it in; `meta` and `ai` held none. The army had lost a battle on the way and the extension turned its task off ("task off: lost a battle"), and the save carried its new data, `enabled: false`. Loaded again: "data for 1 extension(s)" and "restored 0 task(s)", as that data says.
- **Ironman (binary)**: a copy of the ironman save `Castilla-5` (1470.3.13, salted with `I_am_such_a_cheater` as it was) with the entry in binary before the checksum, holding a task for the army in Constantinople (`28136330756150`, center 151, radius 8) and the same `rh-filler`. Loading it: "data for 2 extension(s)", `rh-filler` dropped, the save the game makes while loading an ironman game wrote the data, and "restored 1 task(s)". In the rewritten file the entry lay just before the checksum token, which took it in. Leaving for the main menu wrote it again; loaded again: "data for 1 extension(s)", the save while loading wrote it, "restored 1 task(s)".
- **The game's own check** returned 0 at every load. The trace of `0x14e3390` showed that the check salts with an 8-byte string, while every save loaded was salted with `I_am_such_a_cheater` (the test copies by the script, `Castilla-5` as it was) or, by its name, made by 1.37.4 (a copy of the player's untouched ironman save `Byzantium-37_4-1`); and once a check had failed, every save the game wrote was salted with `I_am_such_a_cheater` too, ironman ones included. That the playset's other four mods turned achievements off, as first supposed, is wrong: all four hold only graphics and localisation, and the run below found a new game's saves passing the check.
- **Not seen in the running game**: the regional-combat task turned on by its button; cloud saves; multiplayer, which is not supported; vanilla loading a save with the entry; data of 16 MiB.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process each time it had to start fresh, and at the end. `tools/player-state.ps1 -Restore` listed what the run changed (the autosaves, the three test saves and two `_Backup` files, the framework's logs, `continue_game.json`, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`), put each back and found every file as the backup had it. No crash folder was made. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies; the regional-combat repository has no change. The backups, scripts, copies and screenshots of the run were deleted.

## 2026-11-02 The game's checksum check with extension save data (tested by me)

Whether a save carrying extension save data passes the check that decides whether a save was changed, which the run above could not see. One run of `eu4.exe` as above, with the same build, tracing `0x97500` and `0x14e3390`; the same playset, `compress_autosave=no`. Backups as AGENTS.md says, with the framework files installed in the game folder copied aside first. No input was sent to the game's window.

- **A new game**: `menu.start CAS`, run to the autosave of 1445.1.1. The game took its checksum with an 8-byte salt, not `I_am_such_a_cheater`. Loaded again, the check returned 1, both times it ran.
- **A save with the entry and the right checksum**: a copy of that autosave with the entry before the AI (regional-combat's task for the army in Asturias, `850403524662`), its checksum left wrong at first. Loading it, the check returned 0 and logged the digest it expected; that digest was written into the copy as its checksum. Loaded again: the check returned 1, "data for 1 extension(s)", "restored 1 task(s)".
- **A save the game wrote with the entry**: from there to the autosave of 1445.7.1, "wrote the data of 1 extension(s)", and the checksum taken after it with the same 8-byte salt. The entry lay between `idea_dates` and `ai`. Loaded again: the check returned 1, both times, "data for 1 extension(s)", "restored 1 task(s)", nothing in `error.log`.
- **Not seen in the running game**: an ironman game that keeps achievements on (`menu.start` starts a normal game; an ironman one is started from the setup's own controls), though the same check decides it and the ironman form's checksum took the entry in, in the run above.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process each time it had to start fresh, and at the end. `tools/player-state.ps1 -Restore` listed what the run changed (the autosaves, the test save, the framework's logs, `continue_game.json`, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`), put each back and found every file as the backup had it. No crash folder was made. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies; the regional-combat repository has no change. The backups, the script and the copies of the run were deleted.

## 2026-11-02 Leaving a session without a restart (tested by me)

S1 to S4 of [sessions.md](sessions.md), after the build, the tests, clippy and the offline checks against `eu4.exe` passed; two defects found in the run were fixed and the build installed again between runs. `eu4.exe` was started directly five times, with the playset as installed (the Chinese one) and the regional-combat extension loaded, and driven by `eu4-harmony game` and `tools/game-input.ps1`. Backups as AGENTS.md says, with the framework files installed in the game folder copied aside first. The player's save manager, EU4SaveMaster.exe, ran throughout.

- **An in-game load from remote control**: `menu.load autosave` (the Ethiopian autosave of 1445.1.1, 36 MB of plain text) from a game of England: "session ending (reason 1)", the game's load, the new session answered after 7.4 s. Into an ironman save from a plain game: 8.1 s, nothing saved first.
- **The first in-process return**: `menu.return` answered `main_menu` after 1.2 s, from the same process. Three things were left: the map manager's minimap, on the main menu's corner; the session's armies and fleets, drawn on the main menu's map; and no continue box. The minimap is now let go before the switch, and the session taken down by the in-game load's world reset, which leaves the history unread. After that, a return from an ironman game of England took 2.8 s (its ironman exit save 0.5 s, the reset 1.9 s), and the main menu looked as after a start: no minimap, no units, the continue box offering England 1448.3.1, the save just written. Why the continue box was missing before was not looked into.
- **From the returned main menu**: loading a save first read the history as the single player setup was entered (2.9 to 4.3 s), then loaded it, 6.3 to 9 s in all. `menu.start FRA` was refused at first, "FRA has no land at the start date": the world had no history yet. Fixed: until the setup is entered the land is not looked at. Then 8.4 s.
- **A new game from a session**: `menu.start FRA` from a game answered at once with the session it was leaving; the game did start as France 8 s later. Fixed: a start not yet run waits. From Castile's ironman game, `menu.start TUR` then took 14.6 s: the ironman exit save, the return, the history, the start; answered with the new game.
- **Ironman**: from Castile's ironman game, `menu.load England-1` wrote Castile's ironman exit save (the file rewritten), then loaded. The save the game makes as it sets up a loaded ironman session then failed: "备份文件失败", and `error.log` "Failed to rename save games/Castilla-5.eu4 … Back up failed", "tried to modify a file the OS needs", a `.tmp` left; the load still held the file (research/main-menu.md). Fixed: that save is made once the load has returned. Then `England-1.eu4` was rewritten with nothing in `error.log`.
- **Undo**: with "save an ironman game before an in-game load" turned off in the settings window, Castile's ironman game was run 10 days to 1470.3.23 and `Castilla-5` loaded again in the game: the file was left as it was, and the session was at 1470.3.13.
- **The player's way**: in an ironman game the in-game menu's load button was on; the load window listed the saves; the question read "读取存档 英格兰1444_11_11？当前的铁人游戏会先保存。" with the setting on, and the game's own "你确定读取游戏存档 英格兰1444_11_11 并中止当前游戏？" with it off. Accepted, the load took the framework's steps.
- **The settings window**: the three toggles showed, on; switching one and saving wrote `save_ironman_before_load=no` and took effect at once; the old `text_appearance` line was dropped.
- **20 rounds** of a return, a load from the main menu, an in-game load and a new game from a session: 80 answers, all right, no crash. Handles stayed between 1043 and 1064. Private bytes grew from 4,094 to 4,825 MB, about 30 MB a round. Then, in the same process, 20 of the game's own in-game loads grew it by 98 MB (5 MB each), and 10 returns each followed by a load from the main menu by 107 MB (11 MB each, two world resets).
- **Against a new process**: France started after a return, and in two new processes, read through `country.get` (20 countries), `province.get` (13 provinces) and `army.list`. The provinces and armies were the same in all three; the countries' treasury, prestige and rivals differed between the two new processes too (chosen at random at the start). After the return, Poland's max manpower was 26,430 against 26,305 in both new processes (manpower 8,632 against 8,631): something the resets leave behind, not looked into yet.
- **Not seen in the running game**: returning with one of the game's windows open, during an autosave, while observing, or from the end-of-game summary; an extension's `on_session_ended` (the regional-combat extension does not implement it; the core logged "session ending" each time); `menu.return` with "return without restarting" off; a failing ironman exit save; cloud saves; the still notice, which is not built.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process each time. `tools/player-state.ps1 -Restore` listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs and settings, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`, `Castilla-5.eu4` and its backup, `England-1.eu4`, and `England-1_Backup.eu4` added), put each back, removed the added file and found every file as the backup had it. No crash folder was made. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies. The scripts, copies and screenshots of the run were deleted.

## 2026-11-02 What an in-process return leaves behind (tested by me)

The run above left Poland's max manpower unexplained. This run looked at the whole game state after a return instead of what remote control reads, and timed each world reset. The build was the one above with the still notice, the time and memory of each reset in the log, and the history no longer read when the single player setup is entered for a save; built with `trace`. Two processes, `eu4.exe` started directly, the same playset and extension, driven by `eu4-harmony game`; backups as AGENTS.md says, the framework files copied aside first. At each point a save was written with the console's `autosave` (plain text) and copied aside, and the world object (`[0x233fe78]`, 0x7600 bytes) was read from outside the process.

- **Poland is no leftover**: a new game as France in a new process gave Poland's max manpower 26,430 in the first process and 26,305 in the second, as the run above had in its two. After a return from that France game, 26,430; after a return from Austria's game of 1724, 26,305; from the Netherlands' of 1592, 26,430. Poland starts with a regency whose dynasty is chosen at random, and its whole block differs between new starts in rivals, enemies, AI personality, mercenary leaders and income; its 22 provinces were the same in all saves. Which random choice moves the manpower was not looked for.
- **Left behind by a session**: compared section by section, a new game after the 1724 game had `hre_religion_status=2` against 0 after a new start or a trivial session, and that game's `achievement={ ... }` list, which a new start does not have. In the world object, `+0x2418`, which the game's loader and saver read and write as `hre_religion_status`, was 2 after both late sessions and 0 otherwise. The empire, the Celestial Empire, the great powers, the religions, the global flags and the revolution were as after a new start; the countries, provinces, trade and advisors differed as much between two new starts as after a return, so a leftover hidden among them would not show this way.
- **The gameplay options were put back to their defaults**: after every return, even from a game just started, the save's `gameplaysettings` differed from the player's `gameplaysettings.txt` in three of its 32 values, which a new start had as the file has them. The single player setup reads that file into the world (`+0x2140`) when it is made, with the main menu; at startup that is after the world reset, but after an in-process return the new main menu is made before the setup's own reset with the history, which comes after it (research/main-menu.md).
- **Where the time goes**: the reset at the return took 2.38 to 2.89 s, of which the country tags and database (`0x207310`) 1.73 s and the world's own reset (`0x749020`) 0.14 s; the reset with the history as the setup was entered took 2.98 to 4.78 s, of which the same country tags step 2.30 s and the history with what follows it 1.95 s. A return then a new game so sets the country tags up twice. Returns answered after 3.3 to 4.8 s, ironman ones with their exit save (1.1 s for the 1724 game).
- **Memory**: the framework's heap stayed at 1.9 MB from the first return on. Each reset gave memory back (private bytes 13 to 147 MB less) and each load took it (55 to 138 MB); at each return the process had 3,994, 4,090, 4,098 and 4,202 MB, with two large saves loaded in between. So the growth is the game's; whose part of the game was not looked for.
- **The still notice**: shown at each return ("正在返回主菜单……", over the game), at each in-game load and as the setup read the history ("正在准备新游戏……", over the main menu), in the middle of the screen with the rest dimmed: a screenshot taken while the notice showed was half as bright in the corner as the main menu after it. Gone at the next frame.
- **Not seen in the running game**: a load from the main menu after a return, which now skips the history (only in-game loads and new games were run); the odd moments, a failing ironman exit save and the setting off, as in the run above.
- **The trace tool**: tracing `0x9f9500` (with 15 other functions, through `EU4HARMONY_TRACE`) stopped the second process at startup, as the trace began writing the arguments of its first call; the game wrote a crash folder and did not answer. Started again without it, the game ran.
- **Cleanup**: `-Restore` listed what the run changed (the framework's logs, `continue_game.json`, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, the autosaves, `Austria-37_4-1.eu4` and `Castilla-4.eu4` with their backups, the second added, and `Castilla-4.tmp`, which an ironman save deleted), put each back, removed the added file and the crash folder of the trace, and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal.

## 2026-11-02 Returning with the history, and what the game leaves (tested by me)

After the run above: the world reset at the return now reads the history, and the main menu is made after it (ADR return-to-the-main-menu-in-process); the framework puts back after every world reset what it leaves, and lets go at the map manager's destructor what that leaves; the single player setup's back button returns in process. Built with `trace`, installed between runs, ten processes in all; backups as AGENTS.md says, the framework files copied aside first. Driven by `eu4-harmony game` and `tools/game-input.ps1`; the GUI root's elements counted from outside the process.

- **Against a new process**: France started after a return from Austria's game of 1724 (`Austria-37_4-1`, ironman): its first save differed from two new starts' only where those differ between themselves; `hre_religion_status=0`, no `achievement` list, the gameplay options as in `gameplaysettings.txt`. Before the fix of the religions it was still 2: the Empire's religions were the 1724 game's (research/main-menu.md). `英格兰1444_11_11` loaded from the main menu after a return and in a new process gave the same first save, of the same size, but for `multiplayer_random_seed` and `campaign_id`.
- **Times**: a return 4.9 to 8.2 s, ironman ones with their exit save (with the census on, which slows the game); the world reset with the history 2.8 to 5.9 s of it; a new game from the returned main menu 4.2 to 8.5 s.
- **The single player setup's back button**: back to the main menu in the same process after 5.4 s (its reset with the history), the continue box offering the last save.
- **Odd moments**: with the court window and an event open, while observing (`observe`), and 0.55 s after the console's `autosave`: each returned, the main menu as after a start; the autosave was complete. From the end-of-game summary of a save dated 1821.1.1 run past the end date, by its "退出": returned in process.
- **The game's own way back crashed**: Esc, "返回主菜单", "确定" shows the end-of-game summary; its "退出" returned, and then the game crashed in the main menu (once of three; `0xc0000005` at `0x15ae44c`, the summary's quit button calling its delegate in the freed map manager). The previous run had only used `menu.return`, which skips the summary. Letting the session's interface go before the idler switch crashed at the switch; at the destructor's start, letting the summary go corrupted the heap (`0xc0000374`) after a return from it, since the world reset had let it go as a popup; letting the two plain structs go crashed later. As built: three returns by the summary in a row, the mouse moved over where its button was and over the main menu's buttons, no crash; then two `menu.return`, a return by the summary, in-game loads of an ironman and a plain save, an ironman return, the setup's back button and a new game, in one process, no crash.
- **A failing ironman exit save**: with `Castilla-5.eu4` held open by another process, `menu.return` answered `rejected` after 0.7 s ("the ironman exit save faulted": the game's save raised a C++ exception, `0xe06d7363`, out of `0x5cc160`), the session stayed, and the notice showed ("铁人存档没有保存成功……"). Once the file was free, the return wrote it and went on; the `.tmp` the failure left was deleted by the game's next save.
- **The setting off**: `menu.return` answered `ending` at once and a new process came up, as in vanilla.
- **What stays**: the GUI root had 3,095 elements in a new process's main menu, and about 1,030 more after each return (1,600 before the framework let the session's interface go). The census put 8 to 10 MB of the game's heap blocks left by each return, and 4.3 MB by each of the game's own in-game loads, the same forty sites every time (research/main-menu.md); the framework's heap stayed flat.
- **Poland**: a new game's max manpower for Poland came out 26,305 or 26,430 in new processes as after returns.
- **Not seen in the running game**: the 20 rounds with this build; an extension's `on_session_ended`; cloud saves.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process; the hung ones (stopped in the game's crash handler) too. `-Restore` listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs and settings, `continue_game.json`, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, the autosaves, `Austria-37_4-1.eu4` and `Castilla-5.eu4` with their backups, and the test save `endtest.eu4` added), put each back, removed the added file and three crash folders, and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal. Two process dumps written for the hangs were deleted.

## 2026-11-02 The country arrays a world reset leaks (tested by me)

The framework now frees, as a country's destructor begins, the array the destructor leaves (research/main-menu.md). One process, built with `trace`, `EU4HARMONY_CENSUS` set; backups as AGENTS.md says, the framework files copied aside first.

- **The leak**: France started, then six in-game loads of `英格兰1444_11_11`: each load left about 26,050 blocks, 3.6 to 3.7 MB, that no later reset let go, against 27,450 blocks and 4.3 MB in the run above; `0x283d8d` was in no generation's leftovers. Nothing was logged about an array not freed.
- **Leaving still works**: after the loads, two returns, a new game, a load of `Castilla-5` from the main menu and a return from it, in the same process, no crash.
- **The leaders**: the world's vector at `+0x18` held no leaders in these sessions. Not fixed; where they come from is in the next entry.
- **Cleanup**: `-Restore` listed what the run changed (the framework's logs, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`, `Castilla-5.eu4` and its backup), put each back and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal.

## 2026-11-02 Where the leaders a load leaves come from (tested by me)

An experiment, not kept: the census module observed `CLeader::CreateFromReader` by call site, the leader's destructor and the countries' history records holding a leader, and, with an environment variable, skipped the world reset's first call (`0x20fa30`, which empties the game's registry of objects by ID) to make it after the world's own reset instead. Four processes, `trace` build, the census on; backups as AGENTS.md says.

- **Counts**: each load of `英格兰1444_11_11` made about 3,160 leaders: 2,275 by the mercenary companies' reader, 806 by the rebel factions', 40 by the countries' history, 38 by a fourth; about 44 leaders were destroyed. The history records destroyed (810, then 40 a load) all found their leader by ID and deleted it.
- **The registry emptied last**: loads and returns went on as before, no crash; each load left 26,090 blocks, 3.7 MB, and the leaders as before. So the registry's being emptied first is not why they stay (research/main-menu.md).
- **Two experiment builds stopped the game**: an observer without `crate::guarded` panicked (`0xc0000409` in the core, twice), and one using addresses no dependency point checks was ended by the framework's own check, as designed. Both mistakes of the experiment.
- **Cleanup**: the experiment's code was taken out of `core/src/census.rs`. `-Restore` listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`), put each back and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal.

## 2026-11-02 The companies' leaders and the rebel factions a world reset leaks (tested by me)

The repairs (`core/src/repairs.rs`): a mercenary company's leader deleted as the company's destructor begins, and every country's rebel factions deleted as the world's clear begins; the framework's other fixes of the game's defects moved into the same module. Seven processes, `trace` build, the census on; backups as AGENTS.md says, the framework files copied aside first. Driven by `eu4-harmony game`. Saves: `英格兰1444_11_11` and `法兰西1495_11_19` (no ironman), time run at speed 5.

- **The first build** deleted each country's factions at that country's clear. One process: a load from the main menu, three in-game loads of `英格兰1444_11_11`, two of `法兰西1495_11_19`, 90 days, a return, a load. Each reset deleted 2,275 companies' leaders and 806 factions (英格兰), 2,375 and 1,056 (法兰西), 2,377 and 1,053 (法兰西 after 90 days); the census found `0x7528c3` gone and `0x861d41` down to 34 to 158 blocks, a load leaving about 16,400 blocks, 2.7 to 3.2 MB (it was 26,050 and 3.6 MB).
- **A leader held twice**: the game's own records of a leader (a country's history) are destroyed late in each country's clear; a faction whose leader went over to another country could lose it to that country's clear first. An experiment build checked each leader before the framework deleted it (its class, and the registry finding it by its ID): of 543 and 551 the factions held, none had gone. The build kept deletes the factions before any country is cleared, as the world's clear marks the world as being cleared (research/main-menu.md).
- **The kept build**: 法兰西 loaded, 90 and then 30 days run, an in-game load of 英格兰: that reset deleted 2,377 leaders and 1,058 factions, with the rebels' armies still there, and went on; 英格兰 loaded, a return (2,275 and 806), no crash until the load that followed.
- **A return after time ran crashed** in all six runs that did one: after 法兰西 ran 21 to 120 days, a return and a load from the main menu crashed the game (`0xc0000005` in `d3d9.dll`, under `SetTexture`) in the main menu or in the loaded session's first frames; the game's crash handler then hung, and the process was ended. A sprite the session left in the GUI root was drawn with its sprite object freed (research/main-menu.md). With the companies' and factions' repairs off, with the session interface's repair off, and with the still notice off (one run each), it crashed the same: not this change. Open.
- **`menu.load` answered "only in the main menu"** twice in a game, right after a `time.advance` had answered, and worked when asked again: the race seen before after `menu.start`. Open.
- **Not seen in the running game**: a country's clear outside a world reset (`0x75d1e0`), whose factions the framework leaves; ironman saves; the 20 rounds.
- **Cleanup**: the experiment builds' code (the checks before deleting, the switches to turn each repair and the still notice off, the census of history records) was taken out. `-Restore` listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs, `continue_game.json`, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`, the three autosaves), put each back, removed six crash folders, and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal. A process dump written for the first hang was deleted.

## 2026-11-02 Why a return after time ran crashed, and the fix (tested by me)

Nineteen processes, most of them with experiment builds (`trace`, the census on): dumps of the GUI root's elements and of the render tree by name and class from inside the game, a watch on the heap blocks of the sprites and their sprite types with the stack that freed them, a check of every drawn sprite whose sprite type was unloaded, and an observer of the show, hide, load and unload of one sprite type. Backups as AGENTS.md says, the framework files copied aside first. Driven by `eu4-harmony game`. Saves: `法兰西1495_11_19`, `英格兰1444_11_11`, and a test save made from `法兰西1445_02_20` with its `meta` dated 1821.1.1, as before.

- **Where it went wrong**: not the sprites' objects, which were all alive at the main menu and at the next session's start; the texture their sprite type caches, let go while sprites of the session still counted it. The game's `error.log` named the sprite types, one at a time: `GFX_messageicon_bg`, then `MessageSettingsButton` (research/main-menu.md).
- **`GFX_messageicon_bg`**: the in-process return now takes the session's interface down as the in-game load does, by calling the game's own in-game load with no save's name, which empties the diplomatic message icons among other things and then stops. That error was gone from the next runs.
- **`MessageSettingsButton`**: the observer showed the sprite type unloaded by the main menu, made again, with two message popups still counting it. The return now deletes the popups the message windows hold first. Deleting the message windows as well crashed the game in the main menu (their message list deleted them again) and was taken out.
- **As built**: four returns in a row, each after 30 days at speed 5 (法兰西, 英格兰, 法兰西, 英格兰), then 30 days more in a fifth session: no crash, no lazy-load error. Then, in another process: a return after 30 days; a return with the end-of-game summary open (the test save run past its end date); 30 days in 英格兰, an in-game load of 法兰西 and 10 days, a return; a new game as France and a return: no crash, no lazy-load error.
- **Not seen in the running game**: ironman returns with this build; the setup's back button; the end-of-game summary's own "退出" button (only `menu.return` with the summary open); the 20 rounds.
- **What stays**: about 1,035 GUI elements in the GUI root after each return, as before.
- **Cleanup**: the experiments' code was taken out of `core/src/census.rs`, `core/src/repairs.rs`, `core/src/ext.rs` and `core/src/game/menu/leave.rs`. `-Restore` listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`, the test save added), put each back, removed the added save and eight crash folders, and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal.

## 2026-11-02 A sprite type unloaded while shown, and the stability runs (tested by me)

The build with the repair of a sprite type's unload (research/main-menu.md, "A return after time ran"), after the build, the tests, clippy and the offline checks against `eu4.exe` passed; from the fifth process on, with the fix to the load's check below. Six processes, `eu4.exe` started directly, the playset as installed (the Chinese one) and the regional-combat extension loaded, driven by `eu4-harmony game` and `tools/game-input.ps1`. Backups as AGENTS.md says, the framework files copied aside first. Saves: `法兰西1495_11_19`, `英格兰1444_11_11`, the test save of 1821.1.1 made from `法兰西1445_02_20`, and the ironman saves `Austria-37_4-1` and `Castilla-5`.

- **20 rounds**, each a load of `法兰西1495_11_19`, 30 days at speed 5, an in-game load of `英格兰1444_11_11`, 10 days, a return, a new game as France, 10 days and a return: every answer right, no crash, no lazy-load error in `error.log`. The build before this repair crashed at the fourth round's load. At each return the repair kept 32 sprite types loaded, each logged with how many shown sprites showed it: the 30 building icons (`GFX_barracks` and the like, which `macrobuildinterface.gui` shows), `GFX_government_boost_states` (`countryreformview.gui`) and `MessageSettingsButton`. Their counts only grew: the building icons by one at each in-game load, `GFX_government_boost_states` by one with each session, `MessageSettingsButton` by one or two with each in-game load. What shows them is left behind by every session, not at one moment of it.
- **Memory over the 20 rounds**, at the main menu after each return: handles 1,047 to 1,070, no growth. Private bytes 3,792 MB after startup, 4,086 MB after the first round, 4,779 MB after the twentieth: about 32 MB a round after the first, give or take 50 MB from one return to the next. Elements in the GUI root 3,095 after startup, then about 2,460 more each round: about 1,030 at the return after a new game, about 1,430 at the return after a load and an in-game load.
- **Odd moments**, in one process: a return with time running at speed 5; with three of the game's panels open (F1 to F3); with an event waiting for its answer; with the end-of-game summary open (the test save run past its end date); while observing; right after the console's `autosave`; a new game from a session, then its return; a return right after an in-game load; then five times a load and a return of each of two saves, with no time run. Every return answered `main_menu`, a save loaded after each was played, no crash, no lazy-load error. The ten returns with no time run added 10,231 GUI elements, about 1,020 each: what a return leaves does not depend on how long the session ran.
- **Ironman**, in two processes: a return from Austria's ironman game of 1724; Castile's ironman game run 10 days, `Castilla-5` loaded again in the game, an in-game load of a plain save and a return; a new game as France from Castile's ironman game and its return. No crash. In the first process, loading `Castilla-5` again was answered `rejected`, "it began a game of 1470.3.23 as CAS, where the save is of 1470.3.13 as CAS", though the game had loaded it: the ironman exit save of the session, written first, had rewritten that very save, and remote control compared the new session with the save's header as read when the load was asked. Fixed: the header is read again once the game comes. In the second process the same load answered 1470.4.12, CAS.
- **By the player's hand**, in one process, each from a fresh session: the in-game menu's 返回主菜单, its question's 确定, then the end-of-game summary's 退出, from France's game with an event waiting; the summary the game opened itself at its end date (the test save run two days), its 退出; the single player setup's 后退 from the main menu. Each came back to the main menu in the same process, with nothing of the session drawn on it, and a save loaded after was played.
- **The heap census**, a `trace` build with the census on, in one process: eight cycles of a load from the main menu (the two saves in turn), 30 days and a return; then a load, six in-game loads 10 days apart and a return. A load from the main menu made no world reset of its own, so each cycle was one generation. Of the C runtime's heap blocks made in each generation, still live after the later resets: 70 MB of the first (what the game keeps once it has been in a game); 8.2 to 9.3 MB, 46,000 to 47,600 blocks, of each later cycle; 5.2 to 6.6 MB of each in-game load. Of each, about 2.2 MB are 747 blocks of 0xbf8 bytes made at `0x153a61`, where `ai_king.cpp` starts new agents; then blocks of 0x178 bytes made at `0x931693` and `0x93dcc3`, 1,600 to 2,700 of each, 0.6 to 1 MB; and 1,585 blocks of 0x120 bytes made at `0x299db2`. They are the game's: the world reset does not let them go, at the game's own in-game load as at a return. Elements in the GUI root grew by 1,034 with each cycle, and by about 300 with each in-game load.
- **Not seen in the running game**: a return with "return without restarting" off, after this build; a failing ironman exit save; cloud saves; a multiplayer game. What makes a sprite type count was found by its name only: the windows that hold the sprites were not looked for.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process each time. `tools/player-state.ps1 -Restore` listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs, `continue_game.json`, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, the autosaves, `Austria-37_4-1.eu4` and `Castilla-5.eu4` with their backups, the test save added), put each back, removed the test save and the crash folder of the 20 rounds that crashed before this repair, and found every file as the backup had it. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies. The copies, logs and screenshots of the run were deleted; the scripts that drive the runs are kept in the session's scratch folder for the work on what a return leaves.

## 2026-11-02 Glyphs on whole pixels (tested by me)

The player found some characters in tooltips blurrier than others, at interface scales 1.5 and 1.6. Runs of `eu4.exe`, started directly, Chinese playset with the unchanged regional-combat extension, at 3840x2160, the player's framework settings (system fonts, FZPingXianYaSongS-R-GB for text); each went to the main menu, `menu.start MNG`, and the mouse was put on the top bar's treasury for its tooltip, with screenshots through `eu4-harmony game screenshot`. Backups as AGENTS.md says. The interface scale in `settings.txt` was set to what each run needed.

- **Probe builds** (not kept): logged the device's render target, viewport and projection where main text and button text draw, and where button text's texture is shown; what they found is in [research/font-textures.md](research/font-textures.md#where-glyphs-land-on-pixels). At 1.5 the treasury tooltip went through the button text method (358 by 292 GUI units, a texture of 537 by 438), all main text straight to the back buffer.
- **A call trace broke tooltips**: with the `trace` feature tracing 0x1596660 (15 arguments), its calls got garbage widths (a texture of 2147483647 by 468 pixels for one), and tooltips were black boxes. The trace's log showed 12 of the 15 arguments. That run was ended at once; the trace was set for that process only.
- **This change** (590 dependency points match; 105 hooks, 8 writes): the tooltip's line "这是你可以用于征募军队", each character fitted to the system font's glyph at the page's size, free to sit between pixels:
  - at 1.5, root mean square differences of 3.0 to 42.1 before (mean 23.2; only 你, 可 and 募 under 5), 2.9 to 8.7 after (mean 4.8), all eleven at the same place between pixels, (0.75, 0.75);
  - at 1.6, mean 20.5 before, 7.1 after, at (0.4, 0.9).
  - The place they share is the texture's on screen, which this change does not move.
- **Main text** at 1.6: in the start-of-game introduction (the heading font), two copies of 的 in its second line match the one in its first to within 9 of 255 inside the glyph.
- **Not seen in the running game**: an event window, the outliner, the ledger and other main text in the body font; the framework UI; a bitmap font, which is left as it is (a unit test only); an interface scale of 1; button text whose texture is a power of two.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. The player played between runs, so the backup was not put back, which would have undone that game; the interface scale was set back to the player's 1.5, and the backup was deleted. This branch's build was left installed for the player to look at. No crash folder was made.

## 2026-11-02 Text textures on whole pixels (tested by me)

The texture button and tooltip text is drawn into, shown a texel to a pixel at its own size with its edges on pixel edges (`unicode/pixels.rs`, the matrix changed at 0x1551a73). Two runs of `eu4.exe`, started directly, as in the run before: the player's playset and framework settings, 3840x2160, the main menu, `menu.start MNG`, the mouse on the top bar's treasury, screenshots through `eu4-harmony game screenshot`. Backed up with `player-state.ps1` first; the interface scale was 1.5 for the first run and 1.6 for the second.

- **Startup**: 598 dependency points match; 106 hooks, 8 writes. The tooltip showed whole and in place at both scales.
- **The line "这是你可以用于征募军队"**, each character fitted to the system font's glyph at the page's size as in the run before, free to sit between pixels:
  - at 1.5: before (that run's screenshot), all eleven at (0.75, 0.75) between pixels, root mean square difference 24.4 on average when held on whole pixels; now all on whole pixels, 4.3 on average (3.4 to 4.8);
  - at 1.6: before, at 0.30 to 0.50 across, falling along the line as the texture was shown smaller than it is, and 0.90 down, 29.5 on whole pixels; now all on whole pixels (one at 0.05), 5.6 on average (3.5 to 9.8).
  - So the matrix the game copies at 0x1551a83 is what the shader uses, and it is a plain 2D projection with GUI y growing upward, as main text's is.
- **Not seen in the running game**: buttons' text compared with the glyphs, a texture whose mesh is cut or turned, a button's text with more than one frame, an interface scale of 1, powers of two.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process; `player-state.ps1 -Restore` put every file back (the interface scale back to 1.5) and removed the backup. No crash folder was made. This branch's build was left installed.

An experiment build listing the GUI objects a return leaves (research/main-menu.md, "Which windows stay"), then the repairs of the map modes' window and the outliner's entries (`core/src/repairs.rs`), with experiment switches turning each off, not kept. Nine processes, `eu4.exe` started directly, the playset as installed and the regional-combat extension loaded, `trace` builds with the census on, driven by `eu4-harmony game`; backups as AGENTS.md says, the framework files copied aside first. Each outliner went with 37 (英格兰) or 48 (法兰西) entries deleted. `mapmode_entry`, `mapmode_more_window` and `outliner_header_entry` were in no round's leftovers in the GUI root, which grew by 1,170 to 1,360 objects a round. The census: a return's generation left 41,000 to 44,400 blocks, 6.1 to 6.7 MB (it was 6.5 to 6.9 MB), an in-game load's 26,500 to 27,900, 3.7 to 4.1 MB (4.0 to 4.2 MB); the windows' blocks (`0x155c294`) 484 or 485 a return's generation, where they were 541.
- **Not seen in the running game**: the map modes' panel opened by the player before a rebuild; the outliner with the player's armies and fleets in it after a long game; ironman; the 20 rounds; the process's private bytes.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. `-Restore` listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`), put each back, removed the crash folder of the first outliner try, and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal. The experiments' code was taken out of `core/src/census.rs`, `core/src/safe_point.rs` and `core/src/repairs.rs`.

## 2026-11-03 The sets of modifiers a country's modifiers leak (tested by me)

Experiments with the census (research/main-menu.md, "What the census counts of the interface" and "Who holds them"), then the repair of the sets of modifiers (`core/src/repairs.rs`). Seven processes, `eu4.exe` started directly, the playset as installed and the regional-combat extension loaded, `trace` builds with the census on, driven by `eu4-harmony game`. Each round: a load from the main menu of `法兰西1495_11_19` or `英格兰1444_11_11`, 30 days at speed 5, an in-game load of the other, 10 days, a return. - **What the census counted**: the windows a return's generation held at its world reset (484) were mostly the interface still in use; at the main menu, once it was gone, 75 stayed. A timeline of the interface's rebuilds and the idler switch showed why (research/main-menu.md).
- **Who holds the sets**: scanning every live block at the main menu, every control block left was held by a vector's buffer in a copied container, and the copies by almost nothing.
- **The repair**, three rounds: no crash; each world reset deleted 397 to 2,663 sets of modifiers, nothing logged about a set not deleted or not moved; `0x931698`, `0x93dcc8` and `0x8f4e6c` held no block of any generation at the main menu after.
- **The build with it**, under the game lock: five rounds, then a new game as France and its return. Every answer right, no crash, nothing logged about a set not deleted. Each reset deleted 397 to 2,713 sets. `0x931698`, `0x93dcc8` and `0x8f4e6c` were in no generation's leftovers. By generation, at each world reset, against the build before run the same way: 2.4, 4.7, 3.5, 5.9, 2.1, 4.6, 3.6, 6.0 and 2.3 MB for the second to the tenth, where they were 3.9, 6.7, 3.8, 6.1, 3.7, 6.7, 3.8, 6.1 and 4.1: 35.1 MB in all where it was 44.9, about 2 MB less a round.
- **Not seen in the running game**: a long game, where the country's modifiers are made again many times; ironman; the 20 rounds; the process's private bytes.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. The hand backup's `-Restore` and the game lock's `-Release` each listed what the runs changed (the framework's logs, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`), put each back and found every file as the backup had it; the release freed the game. The framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal. The experiments' code was taken out of `core/src/census.rs`, `core/src/lib.rs` and `core/src/safe_point.rs`.

## 2026-11-03 Why deleting the sets of modifiers corrupts the heap (tested by me)

Experiment builds, not committed, on master after the day's repairs were merged (research/main-menu.md, "The 0x178 objects"). Where the repair taken out deleted a set, they kept it instead, with a copy of its bytes; a thread compared the kept sets with their copies every three seconds, and a free of a kept set was logged with the game's return addresses on the stack. Five processes under the game lock, which backed up and restored the player's files; the framework files in the game folder copied aside first. `eu4.exe` started directly, the playset as installed, driven by `eu4-harmony game`, rounds as in the acceptance: a load of `法兰西1495_11_19` from the main menu, 30 days at speed 5, an in-game load of `英格兰1444_11_11`, 10 days, a return, a new game as France, 10 days and a return.

- **Kept, three rounds**: 13,079 sets kept; none changed after it was kept, none was freed by the game, none was left by two modifiers.
- **Kept, then deleted at the next world reset**, twenty rounds: each reset deleted the 574 to 2,270 sets kept since the last. At the seventeenth round the game ended a second after a reset of an in-game load had deleted 590 of them, its heap corrupted (`0xc0000374`). Windows kept a dump: the main thread was in the load, in the modifiers it works out after reading the save (`0x9f9500`), copying a set (`0x329ee0`) and allocating a string of 0x20 bytes, in the same size of block as a `shared_ptr`'s control block (0x18) or a vector of one `shared_ptr`.
- **Kept, with the blocks they hold watched**, three rounds: the buffer of `shared_ptr`s of each of 13,630 kept sets and every control block in it, 20,330 blocks: the game freed none of them. The `shared_ptr`s count atomically (`lock xadd`, `0x94230`, `0x8ea610`).
- **Kept, with the census on**, two and three rounds: at each world reset every live block the census knows was searched for the kept sets' addresses, 3 to 5 seconds each time. 19 to 227 pointers to 1 to 3 per cent of the kept sets were found, to sets kept at that reset and to sets kept up to eight resets before alike. Most were in bytes the game does not use, such as what follows a short string's end in `std::string`s a set's copy copies (`0x1ee2a0`, at `+8` of blocks of 0x20 bytes): the search cannot tell those from pointers, and found no holder that stands out.
- **Not found**: what reads or writes a deleted set, or what it reaches through one. Each set keeps raw pointers to the sets added to it (`0x8ea420`, its vector at `+0x38`), which a copy of a set copies (`0x8ea2d0`), and the game never deletes a set it made for a country's modifiers; whatever copied one of their containers may point to a set after the world reset.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process; the dump was deleted. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies taken before. Releasing the game restored the player's files and found every file as the backup had it.

## 2026-11-03 Sensitive capabilities (tested by me)

L3 of [lua-extensions.md](lua-extensions.md): `clock`, `files` and `network` from a Lua extension in the running game. A test mod made by `eu4-harmony new l3-check`, its manifest declaring `permissions = { clock = true, files = true, network = ["example.com", "127.0.0.1:9"] }`, its `main.lua` using each and logging what came back; registered with `eu4-harmony link` and added to the player's playset in `dlc_load.json`. One run of `eu4.exe`, started directly, driven by `eu4-harmony game`: a new game as Castile, a few days at speed 5, `menu.return`. The system's proxy was on (a proxy on this computer), so requests went through it. The game was claimed with the game-lock skill, which backed up the player's files first.

- **At the top of main.lua**: `os.date` gave the real date and time and `os.time` an integer; the data folder listed empty; `files.write("runs/top.txt", ...)` made `eu4-harmony\data\l3-check\runs\top.txt` in the user directory and `files.read` gave it back; `files.write("../escape.txt", ...)` raised "is not a relative path of file names"; `http.fetch` raised "waits for its answer, which it cannot do here".
- **From on_session**: `runs/` held `top.txt`; a host the manifest does not list raised an error naming the hosts it does. Three tasks started with `spawn`, each sending a request, and `on_session` returned while they waited:
  - `https://example.com/`: status 200, 577 bytes, `text/html; charset=utf-8`, about a second later; the task's `game.session.get()` then answered with the date of two days on, and it wrote and read back `runs/fetched.json`.
  - `HEAD https://example.com/no-such-page`: status 404, a response and not a failure.
  - `http://127.0.0.1:9/`, where nothing listens: `nil` and `code` `network`, "the host cannot be reached", with Windows' reason in the system's language.
- **Later**: `on_day` on the third day listed both files; `on_session_ended("return")` came with `menu.return`. The report counted four stretches, 1 ms in all, none over 5 ms.
- **Seen on the way**: an error raised by the framework reaches `pcall` as a value whose `tostring` begins "runtime error:" and carries a traceback, longer than a mod would want to show; unchanged for now. The leftover `menu.start` attempt after `menu.return` noted in the Lua host's run was logged again.
- **Not verified in the game**: a timeout (a unit test has one at 0.3 s), a body over 16 MiB, a redirect (a unit test has a 302 answered as a response), a mod whose manifest does not declare what it uses (unit tests), and a host in the manifest written wrongly.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process; the test mod's data folder and descriptor were removed. Releasing the game restored the player's files, `dlc_load.json`, the autosaves and the logs among them, and found every file as the backup had it. The framework files in the game folder were replaced by this build without a copy of the ones there before; see the pull request.

## 2026-11-03 The game console's own font (tested by me)

This way of giving the console a font of its own was given up for framework files the same day ([ADR framework-files-in-the-game-file-system](adr/framework-files-in-the-game-file-system.md), the next section); the settings and the settings window are the ones it tried.

The game console draws in a font of its own, `eu4harmony_console`, a copy of `Arial12` whose path starts with `/`, in the console font of the framework settings (Consolas by default). Two runs of `eu4.exe`, started directly, this branch's build after the build, the tests, clippy and the offline checks against `eu4.exe` passed; the player's playset (the Chinese language mod, whose `Arial12` loads `gfx/fonts/zh-hans-12`) with the unchanged regional-combat extension, the player's framework settings with no console font in them, 3840x2160 at an interface scale of 1.5. The game was claimed with the `game-lock` skill, which backed up the player's files; the framework files installed in the game folder, another branch's build, were copied aside first. Each run: the main menu, `menu.start FRA`, the console opened with the key left of 1 (a scratch script, since `tools/game-input.ps1` has no key for it), `help cash` run and `echo 中文 ABC 0123 iIl1` typed, screenshots through `eu4-harmony game screenshot`.

- **Startup**: 712 dependency points match; 120 hooks, 10 writes.
- **The order of the files**: the mod's `interface/core.gfx` was spliced (18989 to 19178 bytes), the console font's page made, and then the game's `interface/core.gui` (6949 to 6971 bytes, the two fonts' names 11 bytes longer each): the `.gfx` files are read before the `.gui` files, as the console font needs.
- **Pages of their own**: `Arial12` got a 1536x1536 page in the body text font (FZPingXianYaSongS-R-GB) and `eu4harmony_console` another in Consolas 400 at 15.4 px. So a path written with a leading `/` is a texture of its own, and the game read the font's files through it: the page loaded and drew.
- **The console**, first run: the help text and the edit box in Consolas, monospaced, `0`, `i`, `I`, `l` and `1` told apart; 中文 in Microsoft YaHei, the fallback, whole on the console's 16-unit lines. The strength of armies on the map (`Arial12` too) stayed in the body text font.
- **The settings window**, after `menu.return` (an in-process return): 控制台字体 Consolas and 控制台字重 Regular below the map's outline; the console's list held fonts without Chinese (Cascadia Code, Cascadia Mono, Courier New and the like). Cascadia Mono was chosen and saved: the framework's `settings.txt` got `console_font="Cascadia Mono"` and `console_weight=400`.
- **Second run**, with that file: the console's page in Cascadia Mono 400 at 15.5 px, and the console drew in it.
- **Not seen in the running game**: the console in the main menu (the key did not open it there; after the in-process return the console opened in the session showed over the main menu, which was not looked into); a mod whose `core.gfx` has no `Arial12` or whose `core.gui` lacks the console's windows (unit tests only); the vanilla fonts without the Chinese language mod; another console weight than Regular; an interface scale of 1.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. The framework files copied aside were put back and compared equal by hash; releasing the claim put every player file back (the framework's `settings.txt` and logs, `settings.txt`, `pdx_login_cache.txt`), compared them with the backup and removed it. No save was written, no crash folder was made. The scratch script, screenshots and copies were deleted.

## 2026-11-03 Framework files and the console's framework font (tested by me)

The console's font as a framework font ([ADR framework-files-in-the-game-file-system](adr/framework-files-in-the-game-file-system.md)): its definition, `.fnt` file and page among the framework files, mounted into PhysFS from memory, and the console's windows given it in whichever `.gui` file defines them. Two runs of `eu4.exe`, started directly, as in the section before: the player's playset with the Chinese language mod and the unchanged regional-combat extension, the player's framework settings with no console font in them, 3840x2160 at 1.5. The game was claimed with the `game-lock` skill; the framework files installed in the game folder were copied aside first. Each run: the main menu, `menu.start FRA`, the console opened and `echo 中文 ABC 0123 iIl1` typed, screenshots through `eu4-harmony game screenshot`. The first run used a build that tried the edit on every `.gui` file under `interface/`; the second, after the build, the tests, clippy and the offline checks passed, only those that name one of the console's windows.

- **Startup**: 708 dependency points match; 120 hooks, 10 writes.
- **The mount**: "mounted 1094 bytes" with the three files, twice in each run, a second at 1.3 s: the game initializes PhysFS twice, and the framework mounts after each initialization that succeeds.
- **The font**: `eu4harmony_console` loaded from the framework's files and got a 1536x1536 page in Consolas 400 at 17.9 px (baseline 16), from its 14-unit lines: a little larger than the copy of the Chinese language mod's `Arial12` the section before drew at 15.4 px. Then `interface/core.gui` was spliced (6949 to 6971 bytes). So the game reads `.gfx` files and the files they name from a mounted archive, and parses a `.fnt` with no characters.
- **The console**: in Consolas, 中文 in Microsoft YaHei, whole on its lines.
- **The checksum** in the main menu stayed 4c42 with the framework files mounted.
- **Every `.gui` file**, first run: the game's `interface/end_of_combat.gui` is not UTF-8 and was logged as a failed splice, served as it was. The second run's build looks into a file only when its bytes name one of the console's windows: `interface/core.gui` and the regional-combat extension's `unitpanel_main.gui` were the only `.gui` files spliced, and nothing failed.
- **Not seen in the running game**: a failed mount, and a framework font without a page of system glyphs, whose windows then keep the game's font (unit tests only); the vanilla fonts without the Chinese language mod; an interface scale of 1; the settings window, unchanged since the section before.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. The framework files copied aside were put back and compared equal by hash; releasing the claim put every player file back and compared them with the backup, which it removed. No save was written, no crash folder was made. The scratch script, screenshots and copies were deleted.

## 2026-11-03 The AI leaving a coalition, and the heaps' free pages (tested by me)

Where the private bytes the census does not count go, why the game's heap was corrupted at the fifth of six rounds in the measuring, and the two repairs that came of it (`core/src/repairs.rs`; research/main-menu.md, "The heap's free pages", and research/countries.md, "A coalition"). Experiment builds, not committed: one that measured the heaps and the private memory at the main menu when a file asked it to, and could have the heaps give back their free pages; one that kept every block the game freed while a repair's callback ran, filled with a pattern, and watched it for writes; one that counted the calls of the coalition's two functions and how they overlapped. Eight processes under the game lock, which backed up and restored the player's files; the framework files in the game folder copied aside first. `eu4.exe` started directly, the playset as installed, release builds, driven by `eu4-harmony game`, rounds as in the acceptance: a load of `法兰西1495_11_19` from the main menu, 30 days at speed 5, an in-game load of `英格兰1444_11_11`, 10 days, a return, a new game as France and a return.

- **Measured**, eight rounds: each round the game's heap, the process heap and the only large one, grew by 11.4 to 12.7 MB of blocks and 20.5 to 25 MB of committed pages; the private bytes outside the heaps went up and down by up to 25 MB with no trend (1,889 MB after the first round, 1,874 MB after the eighth), the regions that came and went being write-combined (`0x404`), 16 MB each. The heap's committed pages beyond its blocks were 229 MB at the first main menu, 503 MB after the eighth round.
- **The heaps giving back their free pages** at the main menu after each round, by the experiment build: 35, 64, 71 and 119 MB, in 10 to 20 ms. At the fifth round the game ended a moment after time began to run in the France just loaded, its heap corrupted (`0xc0000374`). The dump: a worker thread, in the AI (`0x13afb0`, `0x58aa00`), freeing a node of 0x20 bytes it had unlinked from a country's coalition in `0x3b0220`, at an address not aligned to 16 bytes.
- **What the repairs free, kept and watched**, ten rounds: 1,148,611 blocks, 148.5 MB, kept by the end; none was written to after it was freed. No crash.
- **The coalition's functions counted**, three rounds: in each session of the French save `0x3b0220` ran 705 to 837 times, 662 to 756 of them on worker threads, up to four at once, on the coalition against France each time two did (45, 24 and 27 times); `0x3e3940` 23 to 432 times a session, on the main thread only; none in the other sessions.
- **With both repairs**, twenty rounds, twice: every answer right, no crash. Handles 1,046 to 1,069. Private bytes 3,957 MB after the first round and 4,359 MB after the twentieth, about 21 MB a round; then 3,938 and 4,349 MB, 21.6 MB a round (23 and 24.7 MB with the sets of modifiers let go and the pages kept). The heaps gave back 3 to 125 MB at each first frame after a world reset, in at most 39 ms.
- **Long and ironman**, with both repairs: France two years from 1495.11.19 (its questions answered with their first option), an in-game load of England and half a year, a return; Castile's ironman save 30 days, the same save loaded again in the game, 10 days, a return; Austria's ironman save of 1724 30 days and a return. Every answer right, no crash; the heaps gave back 10 to 127 MB each time; private bytes 4,143 MB at the end, where the same run of the build before ended at 4,724 MB.
- **Seen on the way**: after each return the log says the new game asked for before it failed ("the main menu is open"), though it had started and been answered: the menu's follow of a start it already reported; and `time.advance` right after `menu.start` answers at once without a day. Neither looked into.
- **Not seen in the running game**: multiplayer; the player's own in-game load from the menu; how much longer the AI takes when its countries leave a coalition one at a time, which was not measured; the rounds took as long as before (33 to 46 s).
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process; the dump was deleted. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies taken before. Releasing the game restored the player's files, the ironman saves and the autosaves the runs rewrote among them, and found every file as the backup had it. Scripts and copies of the runs were deleted.

## 2026-11-03 The command log (tested by me)

Whether one observe point hears every command the game executes, and whether a command writes itself as text with the game's writer ([research/commands.md](research/commands.md)). This branch's build with the `trace` feature and `core/src/command_log.rs`, after the build and clippy passed; the offline checks were not run, as the log's points are the trace build's and startup checks their bytes. Four processes of `eu4.exe`, started directly with `EU4HARMONY_COMMANDS=all` (one without it), the playset as installed (the Chinese one) with the regional-combat extension, each a new game as France (`menu.start FRA`), driven by `eu4-harmony game`. The game was claimed with the game-lock skill, which backed up the player's files; the framework files installed in the game folder were copied aside first.

- **Startup**: 713 dependency points match; 121 hooks, 10 writes. The first process ended at `menu.start` with the framework's fatal notice: the log read `0x2357b30` through `Memory::at`, which knows only the framework's own points. Fixed by adding the base itself, as `trace.rs` does.
- **Every executed command was logged in text**: 40,712 commands of 74 types in three processes, none failing to write. What they carry is in research/commands.md: the setup commands of a new game, the AI's, the game's `daily_tick`, and the player's `buildunit` and alliance proposal through remote control. The framework's `army.move` and `army.stop` made no command, nor did the console's `stability`.
- **Three classes ran with `sender=-1 tickstamp=65535`**: `diplomaticactioncommand`, `spend_money` and `buildunit`; the others with `sender=1` and the session's tick. Not explained; the guess is in the note.
- **Cost**: 9 to 10 µs a command. At speed 5, 10 and then 30 days took 2.0 s and 4.4 s with the log off, 1.5 and 2.6 s and 2.3 and 4.1 s with it on: no difference the runs' spread would show. In the first process `time.advance` ran at the game's start speed and took about 2 s a day; that was the speed, not the log.
- **Not verified**: reading a command's text back, which posting a command from its text needs (tried in the next section); a save loaded; ironman; a return to the main menu with the log on.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. The framework files were put back in the game folder and compared equal to the copies. The game-lock release restored the player's files (the framework's logs, `continue_game.json`, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`, the three autosaves), found every file as the backup had it, and removed the backup. No crash folder was made. The logs of the runs are in the session's scratch folder only.

The build after it executes the command alone, and ran the same requests and more without a hang.

## 2026-11-03 Textures' transparency masks (tested by me)

What the census's largest site, `0x950ce` (the vectors' generic allocation), stood for, and the repair of the textures' transparency masks (`core/src/repairs.rs`; research/font-textures.md, "Transparency masks"). A generation's size two resets on was within 0.4 MB of its size later: these stay.
- **The textures loaded**: about 600 a session, the main menu's and the interface's loaded again at every one. - **The quarantine**, six rounds: 688,977 blocks the repairs freed, the masks' buffers among them, 111.5 MB, kept; none written to.

Every answer right, no crash; 0.5 to 5.2 MB of masks let go at each reset; private bytes 4,160 MB at the end.
- **The window holders left**: after each return, the holders made before the last reset still live grew by about 300 a round (864 after ten resets): list entries of panels gone, the country diplomacy view's shields (`countrydiplomacyview_shielditem`, 225), the map modes' entries (216, their windows killed since 2026-11-03), policies, institutions, the Empire's golden bull, filters and others. Of their windows only about 50 were alive (`filter_list_item` 18, `golden_bull_entry` 17, the lobby's chat 9, the navy templates 3 and the like); the rest were killed. Not repaired.

Releasing the game restored the player's files, the ironman saves and the autosaves the runs rewrote among them, and found every file as the backup had it. Scripts and copies of the runs were deleted.

## 2026-11-03 Regional combat in Lua (tested by me)

Its descriptor in `mod\` and the playset were the player's own. One run of `eu4.exe`, started directly, driven by `eu4-harmony game`, clicked with `tools/game-input.ps1`: a new game as France, war declared on England on 1444.12.12 (`diplomacy.declare_war`, a core casus belli), time advanced to 1445.4, `menu.load autosave`. The game was claimed with the game-lock skill, which backed up the player's files first.

- **Startup**: "Regional Combat (EU4 Harmony) (id regional-combat, api 0.2): extension lua/main.lua loaded; 2 UI fragment(s)".
- **The button**: the Paris army selected from the outliner showed the "区域" button in the button area with its Chinese tooltip; a left click turned the task on, "center 183 radius 5", and the button's glow showed.
- **The preview**: Paris yellow, the provinces within 5 land steps in blue stripes, England's Normandy among them.
- **Decisions**: waiting at first; on a later day "attacking" and a move to Bordeaux (174) the game accepted; when that target was gone, "returning" and a move back to Paris, accepted. While the army moved, its orders were locked and the task waited and tried again.
- **The radius editor**: a right click opened it ("区域战斗", "半径 5", the arrows, apply and cancel); plus and apply logged "radius 6".
