- **Cost, not met**: 32,694 stretches, 5,037 ms in all, about 0.15 ms a stretch (the average target is met); but 142 stretches over 5 ms, most 25 to 35 ms and the longest 65 ms, about one a game day: the day's read of every province (`province.list`, about 4,900 provinces made Lua tables) for the snapshot, and the range's `can_step` calls, rebuilt each day. Coarser capabilities are to bring it down (L6's fourth point).
- **Not verified in the game**: a defeat turning the task off, a recovery of an occupied province, a split or merged army; a save written by the native extension (none of the player's saves holds its data: they were written before data went into saves, ADR extension-save-data-in-the-save).
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process. Releasing the game restored the player's files, the autosaves and the logs among them, and found every file as the backup had it.

## 2026-11-03 Regional combat's cost, and what the capabilities read (tested by me)

L6's fourth point: regional combat in Lua, France from 1444.11.11, war on England in December, the Paris army's task on (center 183, radius 5), then 120 days at speed. Six runs, the same steps each time; from the second on, the mod's `main.lua` timed every capability call with `os.clock` and logged each frame over 5 ms (not committed).

- **`army.range` and named provinces, core unchanged**: 17,975 stretches, 4,917 ms in all, 111 over 5 ms, the longest 55 ms, no better than the run before them. Lua's own work was about nothing: a day's decision spent 30 to 55 ms in the capabilities, `province.list` 13 to 29 ms for two provinces (the core read every province, then picked), `army.range` 11 to 21 ms, `army.can_attack` about 1 ms a call (it looked through every land province for the army).
- **Two shortcuts, taken back out**: `army.range` asking a province's open steps in one call, and `army.can_attack` looking first where `army.at` last saw the army. 2,966 ms, 37 over 5 ms, the longest 18 ms. They went around the cause rather than at it, and `army.range` carried the mod's own algorithm into the catalogue ([ADR mods-hold-the-logic](adr/mods-hold-the-logic.md)).
- **The cause**: every read of game memory was a `ReadProcessMemory` call, 325 ns for 8 bytes against under 1 ns for a plain read; and a capability call from Lua cost 5.8 µs before it reached the game, measured with the test host, most of it finding the capability by name and writing the request as JSON text and reading it back.
- **Atomic capabilities, reads in the process, the game's ID lookup**: the mod back on `province.neighbors` and `army.can_step` for its range, the provinces it reads named; reads under SEH ([ADR game-memory-is-read-in-process-under-seh](adr/game-memory-is-read-in-process-under-seh.md)); armies found by the game's own lookup. The task never decided: the decision core refused a snapshot whose range steps named provinces not in it (the mod read only the range's members), a mod bug the fake world could not show, as it has no military access. 18,910 stretches, 3,144 ms, 41 over 5 ms; a day's 452 `can_step` calls 3 to 18 ms.
- **The snapshot fixed, and the call from Lua made cheaper** (1.4 µs with the test host): 19,403 stretches, 2,305 ms in all, 20 over 5 ms, the longest 14 ms. A day's decision took 2 to 3 ms in most frames, its 452 `can_step` calls 1 to 2 ms. The task waited, attacked Bordeaux (174), moved to 4387 and back to 174, each move accepted, and waited while the army's orders were locked.
- **Frames over 5 ms with nothing costly in them**: most of the 20, 7 to 13 ms in one call that reads a few fields (`session.get`, `army.selected`) or in no call at all; frames like them came before the task was on and in every run. Not explained: `os.clock` and the stretch's own timing count the wall clock, so the main thread may have waited or been off the processor.
- **The spread, a sixth run**, the same build with stretches kept as a histogram and the session's share logged at its end (`menu.return` after the 120 days; the session from 1444.11.11, the task on from 1444.12.12): 20,547 stretches, 1,908 ms in all; mean 0.093 ms, median 0.063 ms, p95 0.132 ms, p99 0.424 ms, the longest 12.7 ms; 12 over 5 ms (5 to 12 ms). 0.59% of the session's 321.0 s, 0.95% of the main thread's 200.5 s of CPU, 0.46% of the game's 417.0 s of CPU. The task attacked, moved between 174 and 4387, and returned to 183, each move accepted.
- **Not met**: no stretch over 5 ms.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. Releasing the game restored the player's files, the autosaves and the logs among them, and found every file as the backup had it.

## 2026-11-04 Extension reload (tested by me)

[extension-reload.md](extension-reload.md) R1 to R3: regional combat at api_version 0.3 with `save_data`, France from 1444.11.11, war on England in December, the Paris army's task on; the mod's files changed by a script for each step and put back with git afterwards. Two runs of `eu4.exe`, started directly.

- **Startup**: "Regional Combat (EU4 Harmony) (id regional-combat, api 0.3): extension lua/main.lua loaded".
- **In a session**: the title text changed in both localisation files and a log line added to `on_session`; `eu4-harmony reload` printed "reloaded regional-combat; on_session got what save_data gave"; the mod's log had the new title ("区域战斗 v2") and "saved tasks 1", then "session 1: restored 1 task(s)", and the task went on deciding.
- **Code that does not compile**: printed "the new code did not load, and the old goes on: syntax error: main.lua:328: '}' expected ... (rejected)", exit code 1; over the next 120 days the old code attacked, moved to 4112 and 174 and returned to 183, each move accepted.
- **--watch**: three edits in a row (the new title, a syntax error, the code as committed) gave two reloads, each carrying the task, and one "not reloaded" line with the syntax error between them.
- **After a stop**: `on_frame` made to loop; the framework stopped the extension after three stretches past the time limit; reloading the committed code ran it again. The first run's build said "on_session got what save_data gave" though nothing was carried, as a stopped extension's `save_data` is not asked; the second run's says "on_session got nil: the old code was stopped (...), so its save_data was not asked".
- **In the main menu**: after `menu.return`, the reload printed "no session is going on, so on_session comes with the next".
- **Found, not of the reload**: after an in-process return, `menu.start` began a game the framework did not take for a new session: no "session N started" in the core log, `on_session` not called, the session number still 1. The same without a reload in between. The new game's first session after startup was announced; one after a return was not. Fixed the same night ([the next section](#2026-11-04-a-new-game-after-an-in-process-return-is-a-session-tested-by-me)).
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. Releasing the game restored the player's files, the autosaves and the logs among them, and found every file as the backup had it.

## 2026-11-04 A new game after an in-process return is a session (tested by me)

The build of `claude/session-after-return`, which begins a session at each world reset with the history (an in-process return, the single player setup left). Regional combat as the extension to watch, its manifest set to api_version 0.2 for the run (as master provides) and put back after; `eu4.exe` started directly.

- **Before**: in the runs of the same night ([Extension reload](#2026-11-04-extension-reload-tested-by-me)), France, `menu.return`, then `menu.start FRA` began a game the framework took for the old session: no "session N started", no `on_session`, the number still 1.
- **After**: France (session 1), a return, Castile (3), France from that session (5), `menu.load autosave` from it (7, "from a loaded save: true"), France (9), a return, France again (10), France from that session (11). Each logged "session N started", each ended with the session's timing line, and regional combat's `on_session` ran each time ("session N: restored 0 task(s)").
- **The numbers**: a session begun by a load, or with another world or player, is counted twice (by the load or the world reset, and by the frame that finds the world or player changed), so the numbers rise by 2 there and by 1 for France after France. They only ever rise, which is what extensions and remote clients rely on.
- **Not verified in the game**: leaving the single player setup by its back button, then starting a game.

## 2026-11-04 The heap census through remote control, and a leak run (tested by me)

M1 of [leak-finding.md](leak-finding.md): the developer build with the heap census (`--features eu4-harmony-core/dev-heap-census`) answering `memory`, `memory.leftovers` and `memory.generation`, and `tools/leak-run.ps1`. Four processes under the game lock, which backed up and restored the player's files; the framework files in the game folder copied aside first. `eu4.exe` started directly by the script, the playset as installed; the saves England-1 and 英格兰1444_11_11, new games as France, 30 days.

- **The first start failed**: "census new at 0x1a332d4: observer slots exhausted". The framework's 44 observers and the census's 5 came to 49, one more than the 48 slots, so a developer build with the census did not start since the command layer's observers came in; the slots are 64 now, and the test that the observers leave slots over counts the census's.
- **Four rounds, twice**: every answer right, no crash, each round 37 to 40 s; generation 1 at the main menu, three world resets a round (13 after four). Private bytes 4,300 MB at startup and 4,609 MB after four rounds in the first run, 4,388 and 4,610 MB in the second; about 535 to 542 MB of it the framework's heap, which is the census's own count of about six million blocks. Handles 1,034 to 1,060.
- **The leftovers**, after four rounds: 5.66 million blocks, 1,207 MB, nearly all of generation 0 (what the game loaded at startup). The sites that leave the most per generation, `steady`: `0x1529f85` in `0x1529d20`, 488 KB a generation, 33,796 blocks, none of its blocks ever let go; `0x15a2658 < 0x152a00a`, the same number of blocks, its buffers; `0x155c294` (199 KB, let go elsewhere at `0x1546536`), `0x156acc9` (166 KB, at `0x15bd3d6`), `0x10b59fe` (100 KB, at `0x10b5696`). The history's sites in `0x2976f0` came out `sometimes`: only the generations that read history leave there. These match what the census in the log found by hand on 2026-11-03.
- **A mistake the run showed**: the first run listed one place that let blocks go five times, once for each shard of the census that had counted some; the counts are added up across shards since, and the second run gave each place once.
- **Not tried**: `memory.generation` inside a long game; the player build, which has no memory group (the build and its tests and clippy pass); a run of more than four rounds.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by the script, or by ending its process. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies taken before. Releasing the game restored the player's files (the run's save rewritten, and its backup added, among them) and found every file as the backup had it. The runs' reports and a scratch folder the script made were deleted.

- **Seen, not of input's making**: `army.selected` answered "army panel not open" right after the click that opened it and again a request later, with the panel showing in the screenshots between. And after `menu.start CAS` from a session in which an army was selected, the army panel's top bar ("1 陆军") stayed on the map in the new session; reproduced in the second run. Neither was looked into.

## 2026-11-04 Where the main thread's time goes (tested by me)

S0 of [game-speed.md](game-speed.md): `tools/profile.py`, sampling the main thread from outside the process, in the running game; the findings are in [research/main-thread.md](research/main-thread.md). The player build that was in the game folder (built 2026-11-04 01:31), not replaced; the player's playset, 3840x2160, `eu4.exe` started directly, three times; the game claimed with the game-lock skill, which backed up the player's files. Every request went through `eu4-harmony game`; questions were answered with their first option and offers declined by a script as they came.

- **Austria-1** (an ironman save of 1618.9.2, the player Spain), speed 5: 60 s at 200 samples a second, the main thread suspended 30% of the time, 8.1 days a second; then 120 s at 50 a second from 1624.7, suspended 7%, 9.8 days a second. The shares of the second run, and the samples of the first under the autosave, are the note's.
- **阿尔达比勒1462_08_30** (a normal save), paused 20 s and at speed 5 for 60 s at 50 a second: 16.8 days a second.
- **The game does not repeat itself**: that save loaded again for each run, the same days let pass with `time.advance`, every province and the 20 largest countries read after. 0 and 1 days: the same twice. 10 days: 13 provinces and at least 6 countries different; with `-threads=1` (the game started again with it), 3 and 8. 120 days, five runs (two with the sampler at 250 a second): 24 to 32 provinces and all 20 countries different between any two; 0, 0, 0, 1 and 2 questions. The draw count after the first day, in eight loads at speeds 1 and 5: 8,664 to 8,763 draws.
- **POPS** was read in `pops_api.dll` with Ghidra, not changed or tried.
- **A mistake**: after the game was released, a run of the sampler meant to show its message without a game found the game another session had claimed running (`memory-leak-fixes-ca4945`, a leak run) and sampled it for 60 s at 50 a second, suspending its main thread about 3% of the time; it reads only, so the run's memory was not touched, its timings in that minute were.
- **Not verified**: a vanilla profile, without the framework; frame rates; what any of the changes the plan proposes would gain.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process. Releasing the game restored the player's files (the ironman save and its backup, the autosaves, `settings.txt` and the login cache among them) and found every file as the backup had it. The samples and scripts stayed in the session's scratch folder.

## 2026-11-04 Game speed S1 to S4 (tested by me)

The branch of [game-speed.md](game-speed.md) with S1 to S4, a player build installed with `dev-install.ps1` over the framework files another session had left in the game folder, which were copied aside and put back after. The player's playset, 3840x2160, `eu4.exe` started directly, three times; the game claimed with the game-lock skill, which backed up the player's files.

- **Startup**: 776 dependency points matched, the point in `pops_api.dll` among them (the module is loaded when the core starts). The first start logged "speed: the pace at the fastest speed stays the game's: unwritable memory": the factors are in read-only data. Fixed (the page is made writable for the write); then "speed: at the fastest speed, smooth".
- **S3, first form, and a save destroyed**: loading Austria-1 from the main menu, the game wrote the ironman save again at once, as it does, and the zip's stored method, which the framework had asked for, wrote 61 MB of zeros with a CRC of them; the thread then compressed them to 69 KB, and the framework's reader refused the file ("not a save the game wrote"). The game was ended at once; the file was put back from the claim's backup and compared equal. The cause is in [research/saves.md](research/saves.md); since then the save deflates at level 0, and the thread checks each entry's header before compressing, which a test shows refuses a file of zeros.
- **S3 since**: the same load wrote 61 MB at level 0, which the thread compressed to 7.1 MB in 1.7 s (`gamestate`, `meta` and `ai` each beginning `EU4bin`); the return to the main menu wrote it again (7.2 MB), and `menu.load` of that file played it, from 1620.4.30. A save of the player's (the save window, with "compress" ticked, a normal game) was written at level 0 and compressed from 43 MB to 4.5 MB; Python's `zipfile` found it whole, the game loaded it, and the game's checksum check answered for it as for the save it was made from. Two autosaves at speed 5 were compressed the same way. The test save was deleted.
- **S4**: the settings window showed the group 游戏速度 with 最快速度时, its info mark and the choice bar (screenshot); the info mark's text showed while the pointer was on it; a click on 时间更快 slid the highlight there, and saving wrote `fastest_speed="faster"` and set speed 5's factor to 1,000,000 at once (read from the game's memory). The numbers are in [performance.md](performance.md).
- **S1 and S2**: in a profile at speed 5 neither `pops_api.dll` nor `icu.dll` had samples left; paused, 84 frames a second against 63 that morning.
- **Not verified**: a cloud save (it would write a file into the player's Steam cloud: not done without asking); loading a save the framework compressed in vanilla, without the framework (the game's own reader read them, with the framework installed); the game's exit right after a save; frames inside a day being none at speed 5 counted against the days, beyond the frame rates; a game after 1700.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process; the framework files that were in the game folder were put back. Releasing the game restored the player's files (the ironman save and its backup, the framework's settings among them) and found every file as the backup had it.

## 2026-11-04 Game speed: a cloud save (tested by me, with the owner's leave)

The same branch and install as the run before, the player's playset, a new game as Portugal (`menu.start`), driven through remote control's input and screenshots; the game claimed with the game-lock skill.

- **Steam**: started as `eu4.exe` alone, the game had no cloud: the save window's 云 tab did not open. Started again with `SteamAppId` and `SteamGameId` set to 236850 in its environment, as Steam starts it, the tab opened, with Steam's quota (954 MB, 948 MB free).
- **The save**: the save window's 云 tab, the name `eu4harmony_cloud_test`. Steam's folder for the game's cloud (`userdata\<account>\236850\remote\save games`) had the file at once, 3.3 MB, and Steam's index of it (`remotecache.vdf`) listed it to be uploaded. Its three entries were deflated as the game deflates them (`gamestate` 33.7 MB to 3.3 MB, each beginning `EU4bin`, `zipfile` found it whole), and the framework's log had no line of the save thread: the local saves' points left it alone.
- **Loading it back**: the load window's 云 tab listed it with the cloud's mark of a synchronized file (and 944 MB free); loading it played it (session 3, "from a loaded save"), the framework's data of the save's extension read too.
- **Upload**: after the game's own exit (its menu, 退出游戏), Steam's index gave the file a remote time and the synchronized state, as it has for the player's older cloud saves.
- **Not verified**: the file on another machine. The test save is still in the player's Steam cloud, for the player to delete (the in-game load window, or Steam).
- **Cleanup**: the framework files that were in the game folder were put back and compared equal. Releasing the game restored the player's files and found every file as the backup had it.
## 2026-11-04 What still points at the leftovers (tested by me)

M2 of [leak-finding.md](leak-finding.md): `memory.leftovers` with `holders`, which `tools/leak-run.ps1` asks after its last round. One process under the game lock, which backed up and restored the player's files; the framework files in the game folder copied aside first. - **What it found**:

Its buffers, `0x15a2658 < 0x152a00a`, are held by those objects alone (33,790 pointers from `0x1529f85`). Together they leave about 600 KB a generation, the most of any site.
  - The other interface sites (`0x155c294`, `0x10b59fe`, `0x10b6098`, `0x15e7654`, `0x15e8c62`, `0x15afb9a`): every leftover held by live blocks, most often of `0x14ec717 < 0x14e8904`. These are kept by a structure that is still alive, not forgotten.

  - No leftover site was held mostly by the game's data. `0x1917cf9` came closest, 20 of 153.
- **Not tried**: a scan in a game, where the threads run; the threads' stacks, which are not read.

## 2026-11-04 Where each round's growth goes (tested by me)

M1b of [leak-finding.md](leak-finding.md): the developer build with the memory account alone (`--features eu4-harmony-core/dev-memory`), no census, so that the game ran as a player's build does but for what `memory` reads when asked. One process under the game lock, which backed up and restored the player's files; the framework files in the game folder copied aside first. `tools/leak-run.ps1`, ten rounds, the saves England-1 and 英格兰1444_11_11, new games as France, 30 days.

- **The run**: every answer right, no crash, each round 28 to 35 s (35 to 40 s with the census). Private bytes 3,850 MB at startup, 3,970 MB after the first round, 4,177 MB after the tenth; from round to round between -22 and +56 MB.
- **The heaps**: seven, and only the process's default heap, which the game's C runtime and the framework share, holds more than half a megabyte: 1,855 MB allocated and 2,349 MB committed at the end. The framework's heap stayed at 1.6 to 1.8 MB.
- **Where each round's growth goes**, the least-squares slope over rounds 2 to 10: 17.65 MB a round of private bytes. Of it:
  - 9.21 MB is blocks the game's heap holds for the game, what the census counts;
  - 7.29 MB is free pages that heap commits and does not use, after the framework had the heaps give back what they could at every main menu;
  - 1.15 MB lies outside the heaps;
  - nothing is in the other heaps or the framework's.

  Handles grew 2.6 a round. From the end of round 1 instead of the slope, round 2's jump of the free pages (357 to 434 MB) made them 15.2 MB a round, which is why the report takes the slope.
- **What it says**: leaks of the game's heap are about half of each round and the free pages most of the rest, so finding the leaks (the census) and the free pages (why the default heap keeps them) are both worth the work; Direct3D and the driver are not.
- **Not tried**: the player build itself, which has no `memory`; a run longer than ten rounds.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies taken before. Releasing the game restored the player's files (the run's save rewritten, and its backup added, among them) and found every file as the backup had it. The run's report was deleted.

## 2026-11-04 Where the slow stretches went (tested by me)

L6's cost: the 5 to 13 ms stretches of 2026-11-03 ("Regional combat's cost") spent their time in calls that read a few fields (`session.get`, `army.selected`) or in no call at all, also before a task was on. A build of master (8bca0f2) with a measurement not committed: for each extension's `on_frame` call, the wall time, the main thread's CPU cycles (`QueryThreadCycleTime`), the time in the allocator and the time waiting on the framework's three locks (the game state, the interface, the session boundary); a line for each call over 5 ms and for every 2000th. Regional combat at api_version 0.3, France from 1444.11.11, `eu4.exe` started directly; the army selected and the task turned on with `input.click`.

- **The calls measured**: an ordinary `on_frame` took 0.06 to 0.08 ms and 0.22 to 0.32 million cycles, about 3.8 GHz, so on the processor all the time; about 216 allocations, 0.01 ms in all, and 13 lock waits, under 0.001 ms.
- **Without the task**, 120 days: 42,465 stretches, the longest 0.683 ms, none over 5 ms.
- **With the task**, war on England in December, the task on (center 183, radius 5), 120 days: 30,578 stretches, 2,608 ms; mean 0.085 ms, median 0.065 ms, p95 0.086 ms, p99 0.424 ms, the longest 6.367 ms; 0.84% of the session's 311.9 s, 1.37% of the main thread's 190.0 s of CPU, 0.60% of the game's 435.5 s.
- **The one over 5 ms**: the frame the task was turned on, 6.42 ms and 24.0 million cycles (6.2 ms at 3.84 GHz): on the processor the whole time, its range worked out for the first time and its first decision made; 40,098 allocations, 1.9 ms of it in the allocator; 0.02 ms waiting on locks.
- **So**: none of the stalls of the day before came again in 73,000 stretches, so none was measured. The framework's work, its allocator and its locks show nothing that could take milliseconds; what is left is the main thread being off the processor, most likely to other work on the machine (that day other checkouts' builds and tests ran beside the game; at 3 a.m. none did). That is not verified.
- **Not verified**: what the stalls of the day before were; that load on the machine brings them back.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process. Releasing the game restored the player's files and found every file as the backup had it. The measurement is not in the code.

## 2026-11-04 Who let the leftovers' owners go (tested by me)

M3 of [leak-finding.md](leak-finding.md): the developer build with the census (`--features eu4-harmony-core/dev-heap-census`). One process under the game lock, which backed up and restored the player's files; the framework files in the game folder copied aside first. `tools/leak-run.ps1 -Rounds 7 -Watch 4 -WatchAfter 3`, the saves England-1 and 英格兰1444_11_11, new games as France, 30 days.

- **The run**: every answer right, no crash. After round 2 the run picked `0x1529f85`, `0x15a2658 < 0x152a00a`, `0x152a122` and `0xda635b` and watched them from round 3, 62,575 live blocks of them. Rounds took 49 to 54 s before watching and 49 to 58 s while watching; the framework's heap, the census's own memory, grew from 536 to 553 MB.
- **`0x1529f85`** (in `0x1529d20`, about 488 KB a generation, never let go): 58,842 of its 61,890 leftovers held by nothing at the end. Of those, the last owner let go was:
  - for 29,864, a block of `0x15e7654` (in `0x15e7600`), let go at `0x152aad6` in `0x152aab0`, the code that lets go every block of that site (62,920 in the run);
  - for 2,971, 2,946, 1,679 and 465, blocks of `0x7d34e6`, `0x8ea526`, `0x114cb73` and `0xc9eba`, let go in `0x7d33b0`, `0x94370`, `0x114c730` and `0x114c950`;
  - for 17,010, none: lost in rounds 1 and 2, before watching began.

  So about half of the site's blocks are lost when a `0x15e7654` object goes: `0x152aab0` lets the object go and not the `0x1529f85` block it points at. The rest were lost through owners whose part is smaller, as some other pointers to these blocks are.
- **`0xda635b`** (in `0xda5820`): 4,994 of 5,022 held by nothing; for 2,311 the last owner was a block of `0xda6537`, from the same function, let go at `0x4e2046` in `0x4e2020`.
- **`0x15a2658 < 0x152a00a` and `0x152a122`**: none held by nothing, so no owners to give; the first are held by `0x1529f85`'s blocks only, and go with them.
- **Beside it**: the free pages grew 13.1 MB a round with the census against 7.3 MB without (M1b's run); the census changes how the heap is used, which is why M1b measures without it.
- **Not verified**: that `0x152aab0` is the object's destructor and that it should let the `0x1529f85` block go, which is for reading the code; the owners lost before watching began.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by the script. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies taken before. Releasing the game restored the player's files (the run's save rewritten, and its backup added, among them) and found every file as the backup had it. The run's report was deleted.

## 2026-11-04 Achievement rules, and the save checksum of an in-game load (tested by me)

The achievement rules of ADR achievement-rules-can-be-relaxed, and why a save loaded in a session lost achievements. Runs of `eu4.exe` started directly, driven by `eu4-harmony game`: `menu.start CAS`, a normal game, run to the autosave of 1445.1.1 answering every question with its first option, then `menu.load autosave` and on to the autosave of 1445.7.1. Plain text autosaves (`compress_autosave=no`). The settings were written into `eu4-harmony/settings.txt` between runs. The Chinese playset, whose Chinese Language Supplementary Mod (`common`, `events` and others) and Graphical Map Improvements (`map`) change the game checksum; for the runs marked vanilla checksum both were taken out of `dlc_load.json`. Which salt a save's checksum was taken with was found by computing it from the file with the memory stream's sum (research/saves.md), with eight zero bytes and with `I_am_such_a_cheater`. The game was claimed with `game-lock`, which backed up and restored the player's files.

- **Both settings off, changed game checksum**: the log said the game checksum is not vanilla's, taken as not vanilla's; the save of 1445.1.1 had `achievement_ok=no`.
- **Outside ironman on, changed game checksum**: the same log line; `achievement_ok=no`.
- **Both on, changed game checksum**: "not vanilla's, taken as vanilla's"; `achievement_ok=yes`, salted with eight zero bytes.
- **Both on, vanilla checksum**: "vanilla's, taken as vanilla's"; `achievement_ok=yes`.
- **A defect of the game's, found here**: after an in-game load of such a save, the autosave of 1445.7.1 had `achievement_ok=no`. A build tracing `0x97500` and `0x14e3390` showed the check failing at the in-game load with the same salt the save was written with, the digest it computed not the save's; with both settings off and the vanilla checksum too. Loaded from the main menu of a new process, the same kind of save passed: the save after it was salted with eight zero bytes. Traced in one process, the menu's load summed the save with `0x171f3f0` and passed; the in-game load did not call `0x171f3f0` and failed: it checks a local save through a file's stream, whose sum differs (research/saves.md). The run of 2026-11-02 loaded only from the main menu. The build of that run (`ac48cae`), built and run again for comparison, has no in-game load, and told nothing more, its saves salted with `I_am_such_a_cheater` as the playset then changed the game checksum.
- **The repair**: with the file summed as the save was, an in-game load of a save written with the vanilla checksum and both settings off left the next save salted with eight zero bytes. With both settings on and the changed game checksum: the save of 1445.1.1 `achievement_ok=yes`, the in-game load, the save of 1445.7.1 `achievement_ok=yes`, both salted with eight zero bytes, and no line in the log saying the repair fell back to the game's sum. The repair's stub then went through the shared frame for calling Rust, as the stubs' test asks; the last run, again, gave the same.
- **Not seen in the running game**: the single player setup's text about achievements with the ironman checkbox clear (`menu.start` does not show the setup); a change of the settings in the settings window, which these runs made in the file; an ironman game, and an in-game load of a zipped or an ironman save, whose entries may not be read through a file's stream; why the salt was eight zero bytes.
- **Cleanup**: each game was closed by ending its process. Releasing the game restored the player's files (the autosaves, `continue_game.json`, `dlc_load.json`, the framework's settings and logs among them) and found every file as the backup had it. The comparison build's worktree was removed; the test saves and scripts were kept outside the repository and deleted afterwards.

Over rounds 2 to 20: 14.09 MB of private bytes a round, 7.33 MB of it the game's heap and 6.88 MB free pages; handles 0.2 a round.

## 2026-11-04 Development commands (tested by me)

The commands of [dev-commands.md](dev-commands.md), which replace the scripts of `tools/`, each tried once in one claim of the game, which backed up and restored the player's files; the framework files in the game folder copied aside first. With `EU4_GAME_DIR` unset throughout.

- **`cargo xtask game-dir`** printed the game folder from the Steam library, `E:\SteamLibrary\…`, and every command below found the game the same way.
- **`cargo xtask install`**: installed the last build and the repository's `dev.toml`; with a `version.dll` not ours in the game folder it moved that one to `eu4-harmony\backup\`, and `--uninstall` put it back; a `dev.toml` installed before was removed when the repository had none.

- **`cargo xtask disasm`** and **`offline-check`**: a function, `bytes:` and `args:` read as before; the offline checks passed.
- **`eu4-harmony backup`** wrote a backup in the layout of `player-state.ps1`, and refused a folder that exists and a running game. **`eu4-harmony restore`** of a backup the old script had made listed what the run changed (the saves the leak run rewrote and added, the settings, the login cache, the framework's logs), put every file back and found it as the backup had it; of its own backup, taken later, it found no change and removed the backup.
- **`cargo xtask leak-run`** on a developer build with `dev-memory`: two rounds of 6 days, 46 s each, every answer right; the report's tables as the script wrote them. `--from-report` wrote report.md again from report.json with no game.
- **`cargo xtask crash-dump`** on a minidump of the running game: `lmvm eu4_harmony_core` and `kn 8` in 1 s, the core's frames named from this checkout's release build (`Observer<19,0,1>::call+0x87`).
- **`cargo xtask perf-run`** on the player build: `--reference` with the main menu showing, then `--name menu --menu-only`: the main menu recognized after 44 s, its memory and CPU written to `menu.json`.
- **Not verified**: a whole `perf-run`, with a new game as Ming, and its `--intro-reference`; `crash-dump` on the dump of a crash; `leak-run` with the heap census, `--watch` and `--quarantine`; `cargo xtask profile`, ported from `tools/profile.py` after these runs, never run in the game, only its parts and tie order by unit tests; the game-lock skill running `eu4-harmony backup` and `restore`, which it does once the main checkout no longer has `tools/player-state.ps1` (this run's claim and release went through the script).

## 2026-11-04 Game speed late in a game, the exit after a save, and a compressed save in vanilla (tested by me)

The build of master at `c6bda5f` (the game speed plan's S1 to S4), installed with `tools/dev-install.ps1` over the framework files another session had there, which were copied aside first. Austria-37_4-1, an ironman save of the Holy Roman Empire, 1724.5.13 when loaded; the player's playset; 3840x2160. Frames counted at the application's frame count and days at the date; speed 5's factor set with `WriteProcessMemory` (the game's 0.05 against the 1e6 "Faster time" writes), while a script kept the game at speed 5, resumed it and answered its questions.

**Paused**, in 1724: 44.6 and 49.7 frames a second over 20 s each.

**Speed 5, "Faster time" against the game's pace.** Counted are the runs in which the script resumed the game every 0.3 s, so a message that paused the game cost little, in the order smooth, faster, faster, smooth, 60 s each, four times, 1730 to 1803:

| | Days a second, each | Mean | Frames a second |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The game's pace | 17.7, 19.5, 18.1, 15.1, 16.9, 15.0, 16.4, 13.1 | 16.5 | 20.0 to 24.2 |
| "Faster time" | 20.7, 21.4, 19.4, 18.8, 18.9, 20.0, 16.7, 17.0 | 19.1 | 16.9 to 21.6 |

- In each group of four, "Faster time" was ahead of both its neighbors: 16% faster in all.
- Before those, with the game resumed only every 2 s, three pairs each way round (30 s each, 1724 to 1730) came out the other way: the game's pace 19.3 to 24.2 days a second, "Faster time" 15.8 to 17.8. Why was not found. One pair under the sampler (45 s each, 50 a second) gave 15.4 for the game's pace and 19.7 for "Faster time".
- The main thread's samples in that pair: the ironman autosave 13 to 20% (the game saved every few seconds of play), frames inside a day 16% of the game's pace and none with "Faster time".
- The game piled up its messages: by 1803 more than a hundred waited, each a window of its own.

**The exit right after a save.**

- **Returning to the main menu with a restart** (the framework setting `return_without_restart=no`, `menu.return`): the game wrote the ironman save at 18:01:48.3; the restart's observer waited 1.5 s for the thread ("waited 1.5s for the background compression"), and the process ended at 18:01:50.2. The file: 6,076,089 bytes, every entry deflated, Python's `zipfile` testing it whole.
- **The process ended 0.5 s after an ironman autosave**, while the thread compressed it: the save stayed as the game wrote it, 55,280,102 bytes, deflated at level 0, whole; no `.eu4harmony-tmp` beside it.
- **Not tried**: the game's own exit (its menu's exit button): the input capability's clicks on the in-game menu did not reach its buttons in this run, the game's hover tip staying on one province, which was not looked into. Nor an end of the process in the moment the thread writes its temporary file, which would leave `<save>.eu4harmony-tmp` beside the whole save.

**A compressed save in vanilla.**

- The framework removed with `tools/dev-install.ps1 -Uninstall` (no `version.dll`, no `eu4-harmony` folder in the game folder). A copy of the 1803 save the thread had compressed (6,077,219 bytes, its entries deflated at level 6) put in `save games` as `eu4harmony_vanilla_test.eu4`.
- `eu4.exe -continue eu4harmony_vanilla_test.eu4` (research/main-menu.md, "Startup"): the continue box showed the save, a window with two buttons opened (its text unreadable, the Chinese language mod's text needing the framework), Enter posted to the game's window answered it, and the game loaded 1803.3.1 as the Holy Roman Empire, its treasury, manpower and the map as in the framework's game. Unpaused, 1803.3.4 to 1804.2.3 in 15 s. The game's ironman save then wrote it again, 7,752,332 bytes.
- `-continuelastsave` with `continue_game.json` naming the save, and `-continue=<file>`, stayed at the main menu; `-continue` alone crashed at startup ("Could not open file: save games/"), the crash being the argument's.

**Cleanup**: the game closed by ending its process. The framework files put back and compared equal to the copies (SHA-256). Releasing the game restored the player's files, removed the test saves, the save the vanilla game added and the crash folder, and found every file as the backup had it.

## 2026-11-04 Leftover temporary saves, the menu's save in ironman, and where a save's time goes (tested by me)

This branch's build (master at `a9a0262` and the removal of leftover temporary files), installed with `cargo xtask install` over the framework files another session had there, which were copied aside first; the player's playset; the game claimed with the game-lock skill.

- **A leftover temporary file**: a file `England-1.eu4.eu4harmony-tmp` put in `save games` before the start was gone once the main menu showed, and the log said "removed 1 temporary file(s) left by a compression the process's end cut short". The saves beside it were untouched.
- **The menu's save in an ironman game is no failed click.** In the run before, clicks on the Esc menu's 保存游戏 closed the menu without a save window, and the input capability looked at fault. Tried again: in ironman games (England-1 and Austria-37_4-1, whose headers are `EU4bin`) the button saves to the game's ironman file at once and closes the menu, as the log showed (the click at 19:25:44.883, the save's "wrote the data" at 19:25:45.185, then the thread's compression); in a game that is not ironman (英格兰1444_11_11, loaded with `menu.load` and with the game's own load window, and a new game with `menu.start`) the same click opened the save window. 游戏选项 and 加载游戏 opened in the ironman game too. Two clicks on it, 20 s apart, wrote no save; why was not looked into.
- **Where an ironman save's time goes**, Austria-37_4-1 from 1724, speed 5, 60 s at 100 samples a second (`cargo xtask profile`): the ironman save `0x5c7ac0` was 20.9% of the main thread's samples, about 14 saves of 55 MB in the minute (each compressed by the thread afterwards, in 1.7 s), about 0.9 s each with the sampler's slowing (the main thread was suspended 18.6% of the time). Of the save: 69% writing the game state into memory (`0x14e3f00` → `0x74ff30`: the countries `0x2dc6b0` 35%, the provinces `0x9d6e60` 24%), 24% the zip entry at level 0 (`0x153b9a0` → `0x153c790`, zlib copying the data into stored blocks), the rest the file. Under the writing, the most samples of their own: the memory stream's write `0x171f290` (14%), number formatting through the C runtime (`0x15c230` → `0x1a41f30` and below, 13 to 15%), the provinces' fields (`0xa366d0`, `0xa36ce0`, 13%), and `memcpy` (`0x1a35750`, 4%).
- **Cleanup**: the game closed by ending its process. The framework files put back and compared equal to the copies (SHA-256). Releasing the game restored the player's files and found every file as the backup had it.

## 2026-11-04 What keeps the heap's free pages committed (tested by me)

M5 of [leak-finding.md](leak-finding.md): the developer build with the census (`--features eu4-harmony-core/dev-heap-census`), and `memory.pages` asked once, at the main menu after the last round. One process under the game lock, which backed up and restored the player's files; the framework files in the game folder copied aside first. `tools/leak-run.ps1 -Rounds 7`, the saves England-1 and 英格兰1444_11_11, new games as France, 30 days.

- **The run**: every answer right, no crash. The walk of the game's heap took 1.9 s, every entry read.
- **The heap's pages**: 2,950 MB committed, 2,565 MB of them used by busy blocks with the heap's overhead; 258 MB of pages no block is on; 127 MB free on the pages blocks are on, 107 MB of it kept by the census's leftovers. Of the 689,000 pages blocks are on, 643,000 are more than seven eighths full and 16,600 at most an eighth.
- **The pages no block is on** were walked 10 s after the framework had the heaps give back their free pages (121 MB then): most of the free bytes the heap keeps are in whole free pages, not on pages leftovers keep.
- **What keeps the most** of the free bytes on pages in use:
  - `0xcc2a3` (in `0xcc220`): 541,285 blocks, 14.5 MB, keeping 38.9 MB, nearly all of them leftovers;
  - `0x5e4e4d` (in `0x5dec50`): 89,701 blocks, 12.6 MB, keeping 11.5 MB;
  - not counted (the framework's, the census's own among them, and other modules'): 126,644 blocks, 824 MB, keeping 8.2 MB;
  - `0xbb8dcc` 5.6 MB, `0x8f163` 3.4 MB, `0x7e7db7` 2.4 MB, `0x1cecf8` 2.1 MB; the rest under 2 MB each.
- **Not verified**: the walk counts 2,950 MB committed where the heap's summary (`memory`'s figures, allocated and free) gives about 2,806 MB, so up to 144 MB of what it takes for pages no block is on may be the heap's own: its segments' headers, or the low-fragmentation heap's subsegments, whose free blocks the walk does not list. Why the heap keeps the pages no block is on after giving back its free pages, and whether they are the low-fragmentation heap's, was not looked into.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by the script. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies taken before. Releasing the game restored the player's files (the run's save rewritten, and its backup added, among them) and found every file as the backup had it. The run's report was deleted.

## 2026-11-04 The game unseen (tested by me)

Whether the game runs headless ([research/headless.md](research/headless.md)). Two runs of `eu4.exe`, started directly, a player build of master (5d63c0a) installed with `dev-install.ps1` after the framework files in the game folder were copied aside; the player's playset (the Chinese language mod and the regional combat mod, whose extension loaded), a borderless 3840x2160 window, vsync on. The game was claimed with the game-lock skill, which backed up the player's files. A script in the scratchpad hid, showed and minimized the game's window with `ShowWindow` from outside the game, and measured through the command line: `menu` answered 15 times, the process's CPU over 8 s, `nvidia-smi pmon`, a screenshot at 25%, and `time advance` at speed 5 with questions answered with their first option.

- **The window shows itself again**: hidden 0.6 s after launch, it was shown again at 3.1 s, by the application's Init (`ShowWindow` at `0x14c02ca`, found afterwards); hidden again, it stayed hidden through the startup load (36.1 s to the main menu), `menu.start FRA` (5.2 s) and the game.
- **Hidden**: the game drew, presented and answered as when shown: requests answered in 28 to 37 ms (median, the command line's own start included), screenshots of the full frame in 103 to 158 ms, the graphics card 11 to 38% busy. At speed 5, 180 days at a time in the same game, hidden and shown in turn: 16.0 and 16.5 days a second hidden, 17.4 and 14.9 shown, at 286 to 304% and 269 to 290% of one core. `input key space` resumed and paused the game hidden.
- **Minimized**: requests answered in 86 to 191 ms, 2% of one core while paused, the graphics card idle in `pmon`, a screenshot in 581 ms, 4.1 days a second at speed 5 (one question on the way).
- **The camera ran away**: from the second measurement on, the map was at its northern edge in every screenshot, until the click of `input click` held the cursor. Inferred: the game read the cursor against the active window, another program's, and scrolled at the edge.
- **The first run's numbers are left out**: its hide came before Init showed the window, so its "hidden" was shown, and its time advances answered no questions; it showed the same for minimized.
- **Not tried**: hiding the window from inside the game (the framework has no such switch); a full screen game; sound; drawing skipped; a second game at once; a machine without a graphics card.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process. The three framework files in the game folder were put back and compared equal to the copies taken before. Releasing the game restored the player's files (the autosaves, `settings.txt` and the framework's logs among them) and found every file as the backup had it. The script, its screenshots, the copies of the framework files and of the logs were deleted.

## 2026-11-04 The hidden start (tested by me)

H1 to H4 of [hidden-start.md](hidden-start.md). A player build of this branch installed with `cargo xtask install` after the framework files in the game folder were copied aside; the player's playset, a borderless 3840x2160 window. The game was claimed with the game-lock skill, which backed up the player's files. Each start was `eu4-harmony start --hidden` from the installed tool, with another program in front; a script in the scratchpad listed every 100 ms the windows of `eu4.exe` (visible or not), the foreground window and Windows' cursor.

- **First try, shown**: with `SDL_WINDOW_SHOWN` only left out of the window's flags, the window was shown 0.8 s after the framework started and took the focus. SDL 2 shows a window made without `SDL_WINDOW_HIDDEN`; the window's flags take it since.
- **Hidden**: from the start to the main menu (54 s) and through a game, no window of the process was visible at any time, the foreground window was the same throughout, and Windows' cursor moved only with the mouse of the person at the computer. `desktop.get` answered hidden, hidden start, no sound. The framework UI found the hidden window by its class, `SDL_app`.
- **Drawing and input**: screenshots of the main menu and of a game were of the full 3840 by 2160 frame. In a game started with `menu.start FRA`, `input key space` resumed and paused it, `input.get` answered the cursor held in the middle of the frame (1920, 1080), and a click moved it there; the camera stayed where it was over 22 s.
- **No sound**: the process loaded none of `dsound.dll`, `XAudio2_*.dll`, `AudioSes.dll` or `MMDevAPI.dll`.
- **Showing and hiding**: `desktop.show` made the window visible with the foreground window unchanged; `desktop.hide` hid it again, and the camera and the held cursor stayed.
- **The main menu again**: after `menu.return`, the in-process return, the window stayed hidden.
- **Full screen in the settings**: with `fullScreen=yes` and `borderless=yes`, and then with `fullScreen=yes` and `borderless=no`, the hidden start reached the main menu (58 s), hidden, with the foreground unchanged and a frame of 3840 by 2160; the game's log says it made its device with "Fullscreen=no". The framework's end of a hidden start in full screen was not reached.
- **A crash**: a developer build of this branch with the test switch `crash` crashed at the first frame of a game: nothing was shown, no crash reporter started, the process ended about a second later, and the crash folder had `exception.txt`, `meta.yml`, `minidump.dmp` and `settings.txt`. `menu.start`, waiting for its game, said the pipe broke.
- **Not tried**: `--sound`, a hidden start whose framework fails to start, the game's own error messages being left out, the input device moving the cursor while hidden, a shown start with full screen (it would have taken the display of the person at the computer), the game's own restart.
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process after each run. `dev.toml` was removed from the game folder, the three framework files there were put back and compared equal to the copies taken before, and `settings.txt` was set back before the last runs. Releasing the game restored the player's files (`settings.txt`, the login cache and the framework's logs among them), removed the crash folder and found every file as the backup had it. The script, its records, the screenshots and the copies of the framework files were deleted.
## 2026-11-04 What writing the game state writes (tested by me)

This branch's developer build with the write watch (`dev-write-watch`), `function = "0x74ff30"`, `calls = 3`, installed with `cargo xtask install` over the framework files another session had there, which were copied aside first; the player's playset; the game claimed with the game-lock skill. Austria-37_4-1 (ironman, 1724) loaded with `menu.load`; the game saves an ironman game as it starts, and that save's three calls of the world write were watched.

- **First try, the developer build with every tool**: the game stopped as the watch began, the main thread waiting in `RtlAcquireSRWLockExclusive` under `KiUserExceptionDispatcher`, from `HeapUnlock`. The exception handler's entry in the process's list is a heap block, whose count of calls under way the dispatch writes while it holds the list's lock; read-only, the first exception waited on itself. The process was ended. The watch now keeps that entry's pages, and those of the count it points to, writable.
- **Second try**: the watch began (94 threads, 8,060,495 blocks, 2,651 MB read-only in 639 ms); 90 s later the game ended in `CoreMessaging.dll` (`0xe0464645`, Windows' error report; no crash folder of the game's). The other threads had gone on, and a write the kernel makes into a heap buffer for a system call fails on a read-only page instead of stopping. The watch now suspends the other threads while it runs, and the build has the write watch alone, without the census and its lock. The crash dump Windows wrote (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\CrashDumps\eu4.exe.59096.dmp`) was removed.
- **Third try**: the three calls were watched and the game went on afterwards, the loaded game shown and its save compressed by the thread as always. The game state (`#0`): 94 other threads held, 8,060,390 blocks, 2,121 MB read-only in 669 ms; 25,821,586 writes in 13,172 groups, about a minute. The meta (`#1`): 2,526 writes; the AI (`#2`): 41,710.
- **What came of it** is in [research/saves.md](research/saves.md), "What writing the game state writes": under the countries' and the provinces' loops, besides the save's own buffer, the C runtime's data of the thread, two static objects that hold the value being written (`0x15bf70`, ten copies of its code inline, and `0x2c59b0`), a pointer each country's objects keep (`0x29cd80`), and statics set up once. Not verified: code this save's countries and provinces did not reach.
- **Cleanup**: the game closed by ending its process. The framework files put back and compared equal to the copies (SHA-256), and the `dev.toml` the install put in the game folder removed. Releasing the game restored the player's files and found every file as the backup had it.

## 2026-11-05 Game testing (tested by me)

G1 to G4 of [game-testing.md](game-testing.md), with `cargo xtask game` from this branch's checkout; the game folder held the player's own install, an earlier player build, before and after. The player's playset, a borderless 3840x2160 window.

- **A claim**: `claim --purpose …` backed up the player's files into the claim's folder and copied the game folder's `version.dll`, core and command line tool into its `framework\`; the record kept the script's fields and added `folder`, `player` and `taken`.
- **Starting and stopping**: `install` then `game start` reached the main menu hidden in 50 to 56 s. The first `start` whose output went into a pipe did not return until the game ended: the game had inherited the pipe. With the game made with no handle passed on, it returned at the main menu. `start --timeout 5` ended the game and failed; `stop` ended it.
- **The queue**: with the game held, `claim --wait` as `qa` and then `qb` were listed in that order, and `claim` as `qc` without `--wait` was busy (exit 2). `qa`'s waiting command was ended; a minute later only `qb` waited. The release then went to `qb`, whose claim backed up and copied as the first had.
- **The release**: it put the player's files back ("every file is as the backup has it") and the game folder's framework files ("as they were"), printed how long the claim was held, whether the game crashed, and `kept\`, which had the framework's and the game's logs.
- **A shown start**: `qb`'s `start` after that release showed the game, since the game folder had the player's install again, which has no hidden start; it was closed at once. A hidden start into a framework without it is refused since, and was tried refused.
- **The player**: with `qb` holding a running hidden game, `claim --player --now`: `qb`'s `watch` exited at once with 4; 120 s later the claim ended the game, released `qb`'s claim (its files and the game folder put back as by `release`) and held the game for the player, 124 s in all; `release --player` freed it.
- **A crash**: a developer build with the test switch `crash`, installed within a claim with a `dev.toml`, crashed at the first frame of `menu.start FRA`, hidden. The release said the game crashed, kept the crash folder in `kept\crashes\` (`exception.txt`, `meta.yml`, `minidump.dmp`, `settings.txt`), removed it from the user folder, and removed the `dev.toml` the game folder had not had.
- **`perf-run`** on the player build within the claim: 55.9 s to the main menu, `menu.start MNG` 10.0 s, 0.85 cores paused and 1.60 at speed 5, 1444.11.11 to 1447.5.3 in 120.3 s.
- **`leak-run --rounds 1 --days 10`** on a build with the memory account within the claim: it started the game hidden, and the round took 46 s.
- **`watch` and builds**: a build with a feature while `watch` ran failed, cargo unable to replace `xtask.exe`; with `watch` moving its file aside, the same build went through.
- **Not tried**: a holder whose lease ran out taken over; a checkout still on `game-lock.ps1` beside one on the commands; a `NOT RESTORED` line from the game folder's files; `start --sound`; `kept\` removed after seven days; two sessions in two checkouts at once.
- **Cleanup**: every claim was released with every file put back. The test claims' folders and the results of `perf-run` and `leak-run` were deleted; the queue is empty.

## 2026-11-05 A save's countries and provinces on threads, and the zip's copy (tested by me)

This branch's builds (docs/game-speed.md, S8), installed with `cargo xtask install` within one claim (`cargo xtask game`, the game started hidden), the player's playset; the saves loaded with `menu.load`. An ironman game saves as it is loaded and as it is left, which made the saves watched here; the player's settings made no autosave in 50 days of 1724, and a plain autosave on the first of January.

- **The first start**: the first save ended the game with the framework's fatal error, "the framework used 0x1d92200, which no dependency point checks": the memory stream's class was told by its vtable's address, which no point checks. It is now told by the vtable's write, which one does, and a unit test checks every address the module takes.
- **`compare-saves`** (a developer build with the test switches): the threads' bytes and the game's loop's, compared in the log, the same each time: Austria-37_4-1 (1724, binary) at its load and when it was left, the provinces 22,876,653 bytes and the countries 24,396,272; Hungary-37_4-1 (1548, Georgia) twice, 14,281,018 and 33,745,776; Ottoman-37_4-2 (1496) twice, 9,482,489 and 32,959,578 (32,959,479 the second time, the game having gone on); 英格兰1444_11_11 (England, not ironman) at its autosave of 1445.1.1, a plain `EU4txt` file, 8,685,607 and 24,282,124.
- **The stop**, by the framework's new log line from the start of the local save to its end, the same Austria save loaded three times (five saves each, the load and the leaving ones): with `game-writes-saves` (the game's own loops and deflate) 986, 1233, 1256, 1362 and 1116 ms; with the framework's 431, 438, 437, 447 and 308 ms. The provinces took 35 to 63 ms and the countries 46 to 76 ms on eight threads. The parts of one save of 310 ms, from a call trace, are in research/saves.md, "Where a save's zip goes". A sampler at 500 Hz held the main thread 98.5% of the time and its shares were not used.
- **Saves the framework wrote were read**: each later load of Austria-37_4-1 read the file the framework's threads and copy had written, and the game loaded it. One of them the background thread had left uncompressed, its move refused ("拒绝访问", os error 5) while the game was loading the same file; the game read that file with its stored blocks too. That refusal is S3's, not this branch's: inferred, the in-game load opens the file before its loader begins, and the loader is where a load waits for the thread.
- **The write watch** (a build with it alone) over the world write of the Austria save, the pool's seven threads left running: 53.4 million writes in five and a half minutes; what the threads wrote is in research/saves.md, "What writing the game state writes". The game went on afterwards.
- **Not verified**: a save of a game in a random new world (`rnw.zip`), a multiplayer game's save, the cloud save (which takes another path and keeps the game's code), a save on a machine with fewer than two threads (where the game's loops run), and the game without the framework loading a file whose entries the framework stored, which the framework's own reader and the game with the framework did.
- **Cleanup**: the game closed with `cargo xtask game stop`; releasing the claim put back every player file and the game folder's framework files and found each as the backup had it. The repository's `dev.toml` is as it was before the run.

## 2026-11-05 A save's other sections beside the pool, the save checksum's sum on threads, the update thread woken (tested by me)

This branch's developer builds (docs/game-speed.md, S8), installed with `cargo xtask install` within two claims (`cargo xtask game`, the game started hidden), the player's playset. Other sessions' builds held the machine's processor at 100% throughout, so the times are of a loaded machine.

- **`compare-saves`**, with the pool writing the loops while the main thread wrote the rest of the game state and the game's own loops as well: Austria-37_4-1 (1724, ironman, binary) at its load, the provinces the same, 22,876,653 bytes, and the countries the same, 24,396,272.
- **The bytes, end to end**: the same save loaded from the same file twice, its load's save written once by the game's code (`game-writes-saves`) and once by the framework's; Python's `zipfile` read both. `ai` was the same; `gamestate` (55,051,381 bytes each) differed in 4 bytes only, at 510, the value of the token `0x2dc6` that is different in every save the game writes (the save loaded, the game's and the framework's each held another); `meta` differed in a UUID that changes from save to save. The save checksums of the two game states were the same: the memory stream's sum, which each `b` past 0xffff makes 1, had forgotten the 4 bytes well before the end.
- **The parts traced** (`dev-call-trace`, the game's code): after `0x5ce5f0` the main thread waited 40 to 96 ms in the join of the game's "SaveSessionUpdateThread", whose update and `Sleep(50)` took 60 to 170 ms a call while a save was loaded; the local save's size check, `.tmp` check, backup and move took 3 ms together. What had been taken for the file's checks and move was that wait (research/saves.md, "The update thread").
- **The stop**, four ironman saves of Austria in a row each way: the framework's code 284, 300, 301 and 302 ms; the game's (`game-writes-saves`) 1149, 1195, 1219, 1312 and 1211 ms. With the framework's, the save checksum of the game state took 11 to 29 ms (60 to 65 with the game's), the wait for the update thread 0 to 2 ms, and the main thread waited 40 to 80 ms for the pool where the world has written its game state.
- **Saves the framework wrote were read**: the later loads read files the framework had written (each load's own save, and the leaving one), and the game played them.
- **A save of the player's** (the save window, "compress" ticked, a plain game of 1444 as England, `eu4harmony_s8_test`): written with the pool and the stored copy, the stop 149 ms, compressed in the background from 34 MB to 2.7 MB; `menu.load` played it.
- **A cloud save** (the save window's 云 tab, the game started with `SteamAppId` and `SteamGameId` 236850 in its environment through `cargo xtask game start`): over the test save of 2026-11-04, `eu4harmony_cloud_test`. The pool wrote its provinces and countries ("wrote 4942 provinces and 1380 countries ... beside the game's writing"); the game deflated and wrote the file itself, as cloud saves keep its path: three entries deflated, `zipfile` found it whole, `gamestate` beginning `EU4bin`. The game kept the file it overwrote as `eu4harmony_cloud_test_cloudbackup`. The load window's 云 tab loaded it (session 5, England, 1444.11.11).
- **The background thread's move refused**: twice the thread's move of its compressed file over the save was refused ("拒绝访问", os error 5), and the save stayed whole and uncompressed. Inferred, not verified: another program the player runs that reads each save it sees, EU4SaveMaster, held the file open. The thread now tries again, ten times 200 ms apart, unless the game is waiting for it; that was built after these runs and is not seen working against a held file.
- **Not verified**: a multiplayer game's save, a save in a random new world, a machine with one thread, and the stop on a machine not otherwise loaded.
- **Cleanup**: the game closed with `cargo xtask game stop`; releasing each claim put back every player file (the test save of the player's removed with them) and the game folder's framework files, and found each as the backup had it. The two cloud test files, `eu4harmony_cloud_test` and `eu4harmony_cloud_test_cloudbackup`, are still in the player's Steam cloud, for the player to delete. The repository's `dev.toml` is as it was.

## 2026-11-05 Sessions told from one module (tested by me)

The player build of the branch that moved what begins and ends a session into `core/src/sessions.rs`, the capability catalogue's groups into `core/src/catalogue/` and each repair into a file of its own. One process, within a claim, started hidden; the player's playset, with the regional-combat extension loaded (Lua). Driven by `eu4-harmony game`.

- **Startup**: 814 dependency points matched; 147 hooks, 12 writes; the extension loaded.
- **A new game**: `menu.start FRA` answered session 1; `time.advance 5` stopped at the game's question, which `question.answer` answered; `time.advance 5` then gave five `day` events. `army.list` read the armies.
- **An in-game load**: `menu.load H-Test` answered session 3. The events said `session_ended` with `load`, then `session` 3; the log said `session 1 ending (load)` before `session 3 started (from a loaded save: true)`, with the extension's session times between.
- **A return**: `menu.return` answered at the main menu; `session_ended` with `return`; the process number stayed, an in-process return.
- **A new game after it**: `menu.start CAS` answered session 5, and its days came.

- **Then, in a second claim** (two processes, the same build):

  - **A failing ironman exit save**: with `Castilla-5.eu4` held open by another process, `menu.return` was refused twice ("the ironman exit save faulted"), the session staying and the notice shown; once the file was free, the third wrote the save and returned in process. The log said `session 2 ending (return)` once, at the first try, and the events had one `session_ended`: the later tries were not told again.
  - **The single player setup's back button**, clicked with `input`: back to the main menu in the same process ("the single player setup was left; the world reset with the history"); `menu.start FRA` after it answered a new session, 5.
  - **A restart**: with `return_without_restart=no` in the framework settings and the game started again, `menu.return` answered `ending`; the old process logged `session 1 ending (return)`, "the game starts eu4.exe again" and remote control answering every request `not_ready` from then on; a new process answered `main_menu` on the pipe. The new process was not hidden: the game's restart builds its command line again without `-harmony_hidden`, so it showed on the desktop until it was stopped, about a minute and a half. That is the hidden start's, not this branch's.
- **Not tried**: a frame of a game that cannot be played (observing).
- **Cleanup**: each `release` put back every file the run changed (the framework's logs and settings, `pdx_login_cache.txt`, `settings.txt`, `shortcuts.txt`, the saves the loads and the ironman exit save touched) and compared each equal, and put back the game folder's framework files. The game did not crash.
