## 2026-11-05 Player actions follow-ups: the amounts (tested by me)

The two amounts the parts left unexplained ("Player actions A5", "the parts together" above), on a player build of `claude/player-actions-fu-amounts-3c701e`, within a claim, started hidden; two new games as France at 1444.11.11, driven through remote control only, the second with `remote_full_view=yes` in the framework settings to read the other countries' treasuries. What the game's code does is in [research/peace.md](research/peace.md) ("Gold when the treaty is carried out") and [research/power-spending.md](research/power-spending.md) ("The economy").

- **The loan**: paused, `economy.options` gave `take_loan` 128 ducats; `economy.take_loan` raised the treasury from 66.487 to 194.487 and `loan.list` listed a loan of 128. A month on (1444.12.1) with nothing done it was still 128. Then `province.develop_tax 183` raised it to 129 at once (`estate.grant_privilege estate_church estate_church_land_rights` after it left it at 129); a loan taken then raised the treasury by 129, from 198.323 to 327.323, and `loan.list` listed 128 and 129. So `gives` is what the player gets when they take the loan; in the parts' run together the options were read before `province.develop_tax` and the privilege, and the loan taken after. Nothing in the framework changed; the descriptions say the amount follows the income.
- **Peace gold**: at war with England (`declare_war ENG cb_core --province 167`, no allies called; France and its five appanages Armagnac, Auvergne, Bourbon, Foix and Orléans attacking), after `winwars` and the diplomat's month, the treasuries were France 74.262, Armagnac 35.334, Auvergne 38.964, Bourbon 37.958, Foix 38.02, Orléans 25.035, England 120.097. Demanding `[{"term":"gold","amount":300}]` was accepted: England −179.903 (300.000 paid), France 333.039 (+258.777), Orléans 66.258 (+41.223), the other four unchanged. The game shares the gold among the countries of the receiving side by their participation in the war, the treaty's actor taking what rounding leaves; the peace view's gold term says the whole 300, as the framework's term, `peace.terms`' most and `peace.evaluate`'s cost do, so they were left as they are, and the descriptions of `peace.terms`, `peace.demand` and the term's `amount` say that the player receives a share.
- **Not verified**: who counts as the receiving side when the treaty's lists and the war's side differ (a separate peace, a war leader's ally); the game's switch that gives the actor all the gold (`0x975110`), which was on in both runs; gold given in a peace offer (`peace.offer`), which by the code is paid in whole by the player.
- **Cleanup**: the release put the player's files back (every file as the backup had it, the framework settings without `remote_full_view=yes` among them) and the game folder's framework files.
## 2026-11-05 Player actions follow-up: what the events cost, and the player's view read by read (tested by me)

Player builds of `claude/player-actions-fu-cost-view-4c4534`, after the build, the tests, clippy and the offline checks passed; the player's playset with the Chinese language mod, 3840x2160, the game started hidden within a claim (game-test skill), which backed up the player's files and the game folder's framework files and put them back at its release. Everything was read through `eu4-harmony game …` and `cargo xtask scenario perf`.

**The events' cost.** Four runs of `scenario perf` (a new game as Ming, 120 s at speed 5), two listening to nothing and two with a remote client listening to the seven events; the figures and the framework's timing are in [performance.md](performance.md#the-events-2026-11-05). In all about 74 µs of the main thread a game day, 0.1% at speed 5; the pace spread 16% between the two runs that listened to nothing. Reading only the provinces' owners took that watch from 84 to 55 µs a day. A run that also read `province.get` of every land province stopped at the first undiscovered one, which the player's view now refuses; the scenario since reads only the provinces a country owns.

**The player's view.** A new game as Ming, the start-of-game question answered and three days run; 82 reads, each with another country (Korea, KOR, and France, FRA, which Ming has not met), another's province (Hanseong 735, Korean; Kyoto 1020 and 1028, Japanese, under fog of war; Paris 183, undiscovered) or none, written to files; then `army.at` of each of the 518 provinces a country owns that fog of war hid. The game was then closed, `remote_full_view=yes` written into the framework's `settings.txt`, and the same game started again for the same reads. Between the two starts the game itself differed in what it rolled (manpower, opinions, trade values, which rival came first): those differences are left out below.

| Read | Player's view | Full view | The game's panel |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `country.get KOR` | treasury, power, governing capacity, inflation, mercantilism, loans and rebels null; manpower 9789 of 17902, stability 0, prestige -4.958, overlord MNG (tributary), rival MJZ, technology 3/3/3 | those seven given (power 126/112/112, capacity 102.43 of 400, mercantilism 10, two rebel factions at 0) | the diplomacy view: stability 0, prestige -4, overlord Ming, rival Jianzhou; no treasury or monarch points |
| `country.budget KOR` | income, expenses and loans null | Korea's budget (tax 11.036 …) | not shown for another country |
| `province.get 735` | unrest and constructions null | unrest -11.494, no constructions | the province view: development 9/10/6, fort, autonomy 0.0%, unrest 0.0: the panel shows 0.0, not Korea's -11.5 |
| `province.get 1020` | unrest and constructions null | unrest -8.9, a diplomat's construction by OGS | not opened |
| `province.get 183` | refused: not discovered | Paris, owned by France | terra incognita on the map |
| `province.list` | 4027 land provinces, 263 visible, 3250 with no owner (undiscovered or unowned) | owners for all | the map |
| `army.at` of the 518 fogged provinces | none in any | armies in 171 of them, Kyoto's (ASK, 14 regiments) and Paris's (FRA, 14) among them | none drawn under fog |
| `army.at 735` | KOR's army, 14 regiments | the same | the army's tooltip on the map: 10/4/0 regiments |
| `trade.nodes` | 26 nodes, after the fix below | 80 | the trade map mode showed the same 26, Siberia, Samarkand and Alexandria among them, Persia and Basra not |
| `trade.node venice` | refused: not discovered (after the fix) | Venice's node | not on the trade map |
| `building.options`, `missionary.options` 735 | no construction, no progress | the same here: none was going on | not compared |
| `siege.options` | no siege anywhere asked | the same | no siege going on |

The other reads of the list in [player-actions.md](player-actions.md#the-players-view-and-the-full-view) answered the same in both views, but for what the game rolled.

- **Fixed here**: `building.options` gave what is being built in another's province and `missionary.options` another's missionary's progress, which `province.get` leaves out; `siege.options` told of a siege fog of war hides; `province.get` and `province.list` gave terra incognita as the game keeps it; the trade reads gave every node. The first build of the trade fix took a node as seen when its location province was discovered, and left out Siberia, Samarkand and Alexandria, which the trade map shows: a node is seen once one of its provinces is discovered.
- **After the run**, from reading the code: `army.at` gave another country's army the game's own state (busy, attached, movement locked) among what blocks it, which no panel shows; the player's view now gives only fighting, besieging and retreating. Not tried in the game.
- **Found by the dependency check**: one byte of the first trade dependency point was wrong, and the hidden start ended at once, naming the point.
- **Not verified**: a construction or a missionary in another's province and a siege under fog of war in the player's view (none was going on); the ledger's pages on another country (manpower is not on its diplomacy view); a country the player has not met, which every read answers as any other; extensions, which read the full view.
- **Cleanup**: the release put back the player's files and the framework settings; the screenshots and the read files were in the scratchpad.
## 2026-11-05 Player actions follow-up: armies aboard fleets (tested by me)

The follow-up of A4's transport ([player-actions.md](player-actions.md), A4), a developer build with the command log of branch `claude/player-actions-fu-transport-ba816d`, within a claim, started hidden; the player's playset. A new game as England at 1444.11.11, driven through remote control only, to 1445.3.29.

- **Why A4 found no route onto the sea**: the route's step from land onto a sea zone is open only when one fleet there has room for the whole army: the game asks each fleet whether its ships' room (one regiment for each transport, nothing for the warships in the unmodded game) less the regiments aboard holds every regiment of the army ([research/units.md](research/units.md), [research/routes.md](research/routes.md)). England's armies have 13 and 14 regiments. The route query is the right click's own; the right click then offers the automatic transport instead.
- **Refusals**: with no fleet in the Thames sea zone (1270), `army.move <london army> 1270` was refused "the player has no fleet in 1270 to board"; with the 9-ship fleet there, the 14-regiment army "no fleet of the player's in 1270 has room for the army's 14 regiments: the most is 0".
- **Boarding**: with the 24-ship fleet in 1270 too, the army split into 7 and 7 and each half ordered to 1270 got the route `[1270]`; within 20 days both stood in 1270 with `aboard` the 24-ship fleet, whose `carrying` went to 7, then 14. While the step lasted, `blocked` read `movement_locked`, as on land.
- **Moving aboard**: `army.move <fleet> 1271` took the fleet to the sea zone off Calais in 6 days, and both armies aboard read province 1271.
- **Landing**: `army.route_to <army aboard> 87` gave `[87]` from 1271 (from 1270, not next to Calais, no route); `army.move` set it, the army stayed aboard and counted in the fleet's `carrying` while it went, and on 1445.1.17 stood in Calais with `aboard` null; the fleet's `carrying` read 7.
- **Recruiting transports** (A4's seven in Gwynedd never built): `recruit.order 242 transport` in Gwynedd (格温内斯) took 12 ducats at once (78.4 to 66.4) and put a cog in the province's constructions, started 1445.1.25 and to end **1446.1.17**; it progressed the next day. Five more took 12 each, to 6.4; the seventh was refused "建立需要12¤。". So recruiting ships has no fault: a cog takes about a year, and A4's run, which ended 1445.11.26, did not last until its transports were done. That A4's orders took no money was not seen again.
- **Not verified**: boarding a fleet in a battle. After a declaration of war on France (no casus belli), the fleets waited in 1270 to 1272 for two months and no naval battle came about. The game's step onto the sea does not ask about a battle (read from the code only); what the move does then was not seen. Not tried: the fleet panel's way of landing (the fleet selected, a coastal province clicked), which the framework does not use; an extension calling any of this.
- The release put every file back and the game folder's framework files as they were.
## 2026-11-05 Player actions from a Lua extension, and the new events' handlers (tested by me)

The capabilities of [player-actions.md](player-actions.md) called from a Lua extension, and the handlers of its new events, which no run had tried. A player build of the follow-up branch, within a claim, started hidden; the player's playset with a scratch mod made with `eu4-harmony new`, linked and added to `dlc_load.json` for the run. The mod logged every answer as one line of JSON, and every event its handlers heard; it ran Lua it found in its data folder (`files`) at its next frames, as a task, which drove the run. A scratch program held each logged answer to the catalogue's result shape (`result_shape`, the shapes the editor's types are written from). Two processes, each a new game as France at 1444.11.11; time advanced through remote control, questions answered through it too.

- **Every group** extensions have (38) answered a read from Lua, and every group with actions took one or refused it with the game's reasons: `province.develop_tax` (refused for points), `recruit.order`, `diplomacy.improve_relations`, `declare_war` and `warn`, `question.answer`, `focus.choose`, `economy.take_loan`, `reduce_inflation`, `loan.repay`, `estate.grant_privilege`, `subject.interact` (`placate_rulers` on Orléans), `leader.recruit`, `assign`, `mercenary.hire`, `fort.mothball`, `reopen`, `building.build`, `province.make_territory`, `peace.demand`, `camera.look_at`, `zoom`, `missionary.raise_automatic`, `lower_automatic`; refused with the game's words: `technology.research`, `stability.raise`, `idea.take_group`, `policy.enable`, `government.adopt_reform`, `mission.complete`, `colonist.send`, `trade_company.add`, `center_of_trade.upgrade`, `merchant.collect`, `missionary.send`, `siege.assault` (no fort at Bordeaux), `occupation.transfer` (no ally in the war), `parliament.debate` (no parliament).
- **Shapes**: all 156 answers held to their capability's shape, the empty table Lua writes as `{}` read as an empty list where the shape is one.
- **The editor's types** were wrong for seven answers, which no answer showed: a class is written once, by its name, and six names, `Loan`, `Technology`, `IdeaGroup`, `Interaction`, `Construction` and `Fort`, were each two types, so `loan.list`, `technology.options`, `idea.list`, `estate.interactions`, `subject.options`, `building.options` and `fort.list` had another answer's fields (`loan.list`'s loans `lender, ducats, interest, due`, a country's loans). Each now has a name of its own, and a test holds one class to one shape. The handlers' types gave `on_province_owner_changed`'s `from` and `to` as `string`; they are `string|nil` now.
- **The full view**: Lua read Muscovy's treasury (52.7) and monarch power and the Muscovite army in Moscow under fog of war; remote control's `country.get MOS` gave both as null and `army.at 295` `not_ready` (visibility not current yet) at the same moment.
- **Errors**: a call the game could not do returned `nil, err` with `{code, message}` and nothing else: `not_found` for `army.get(987654321)`, `country.get("QQQ")`, a decision and an offer that do not exist, `army.selected` without the army panel open; `rejected` with the game's reasons for the refusals above, `peace.terms` not at war, `siege.options` with no siege; `invalid_argument` for `time.set_speed(9)`; `not_ready` for `army.route_to` and `army.move` while paused, as documented for extensions. A call made wrongly raised an error naming the argument (`province.get("Paris")`, `army.move()` with no arguments, `building.build(183, 7)`), which `pcall` caught. The wording read "a integer"; it is "an integer" now.
- **Found and fixed: `load` without an environment**: the sandbox's `load` passed its fourth argument on even when not given, and an environment given as nil is the chunk's, so every chunk an extension loaded without one had a nil `_ENV` and failed at its first global ("attempt to index a nil value (upvalue '_ENV')"). Fixed, and in the second process the mod's `load(source, "=cmd")` saw the globals. An error in the task did not stop the extension: its next commands ran.
- **Found and fixed**: `government.adopt_reform centralize_reform` gave "在欧洲拥有少于15个省份。" twice, the line being in the reform's potential and its trigger; in the second process once.
- **Handlers**: `on_month` at 1444.12.1 and 1445.1.1, `on_year` at 1445.1.1 after it; `on_battle` for battles anywhere (rebels against Ansbach, Livonia, Trier, Liège, Münster, Riga, ...), each a table with province, naval, attackers, defenders, winner and both losses; `on_war_declared` for France's declaration on England ("法兰西收复拉布尔之战", FRA, ENG) and for wars of others (Mamluks on Anizah, Hosokawa on Kono); `on_province_owner_changed` 49 and 4747 Teutonic Order to Brandenburg, and after the peace 174 ENG to FRA, `from` and `to` strings; `on_peace_made` with the attackers France and its five appanages, the defenders England, Ulster, Kildare and Portugal, `ended` true; `on_building_finished` 16 `fort_15th` NOR.
- **The war**: `diplomacy.declare_war` was refused before 1444.12.11 ("在1444年12月11日之前不能宣战。"), then declared with the first war goal (`cb_core`, Labourd); both armies were sent to Bordeaux, besieged and took it; after the console's `winwars`, `peace.evaluate` accepted (+100 "被彻底击败：") and `peace.demand` Bordeaux `accepted`.
- **Not tried**: a battle France fought (the English armies were in London and Portugal, out of reach), so `on_army_defeated` was not heard; a building of France's finishing (the castle built in Paris finishes in 1447); an extension's first error raising its notice, not looked at; `on_battle`'s table has no class in the editor's types (`table`).
- **Cleanup**: the release put the player's files back (every file as the backup had it) and the game folder's framework files; the mod's link file was deleted.
## 2026-11-05 Player actions A8 follow-up: trading policies, colonial upkeep, expelling minorities, settlement growth (tested by me)

What A8 left undone ([player-actions.md](player-actions.md), A8). Developer builds of this branch with the command log and the test switches' posts, within a claim, started hidden; the player's playset. A new game as Portugal at 1444.11.11, then a new game as Castile, its autosaves of 1445.1.1 and 1446.1.1 loaded so that the test switches' `set_native_policy` was posted at the load (the game's check takes it only once the country has a colonist). Everything through remote control; the console's `add_colonist`, `add_idea_group` (expansion and exploration, which came in whole and gave two colonists that last), `discover`, `own`, `add_core` and `population` set the cases up. Each command the layer posted showed in the command log in the written form [research/envoys.md](research/envoys.md) gives.

- **Trading policies** (Portugal, its merchant steering in Tunis): `merchant.policies tunis` listed the database's nine policies in the game's order with their names (利润最大化, 渗透式贸易, …), `maximize_profit` current, `hostile_trading` and `establish_communities` allowed, the others refused with the game's lines: the upgraded ones with their country flag ("拥有国家标志'enabled_trading_policy_maximize_profit_upgraded'"), `improve_inland_routes` with "突尼斯:" and "葡萄牙的贸易竞争力达到 50%。", `propagate_religion` with its whole failing condition list. So the policy's trigger holds its `potential` and `can_select` together. In Sevilla, where Portugal has no merchant, every policy was refused with "葡萄牙在塞维利亚有一个商人团", and `set_policy sevilla maximize_profit` with it. `set_policy tunis hostile_trading` was executed (`select_trading_policy={ … members={ dummy="POR" dummy=58 dummy=2 } }`); then `hostile_trading` read current, and every other policy, `maximize_profit` among them, was refused with the game's cooldown text "你必须等到 1445年11月11日 才能在这个贸易节点选择一个新的贸易政策。", which `set_policy tunis establish_communities` gave as its refusal; choosing `hostile_trading` again was refused by the framework ("the player already follows hostile_trading there"), an unknown key was `not_found`.
- **Colonial upkeep**: `colonist.options` gave a colonist's work 0.333 ducats a month with no colonist, 2.00 with one or two (the share beyond the country's colonists falls away). With one console colonist promoting settlement growth in Lisbon, the budget's 殖民地维护费 was 2.00 a month, where the first build had given a twelfth of it: the sum is a month's, not a year's, and the build was fixed. Not as the budget later shows: Havana, given 2.00 before the colony was founded, cost with Madrid's settlement growth 8.00 a month together once it had settlers, and 10.00 alone a month later: a settled colony's own modifiers come on top of what the game counts for a province without settlers, which is what `colonist.options` gives.
- **Expelling minorities** (Castile, the Bahamas given with `own`, `add_core` and `population` as a port within range): Havana (484, a colonial region) listed 19 of Castile's provinces (Andalusian and others), each refused with the send's own reasons while Havana was out of range ("这个省份太远了。" and the range lines); in range, 15 were allowed and 4 refused with the game's "我们只能把少数族群从我们目前控制的省份中驱逐出去。", which `expel_minorities 484 206` gave as its refusal. Arguin and Bermuda listed none (no colonial region). `expel_minorities 484 224` was executed (`send_colonist={ … country="CAS" location=484 settlement_growth_construction=no expulsion_province=224 }`); Havana then read `working`, its province list the game's "这里目前有一个殖民地正在建造中。", and the colony grew with a `minority_expulsion` construction; Madrid (217), Castilian and not listed, was refused by the framework ("the colonize button lists no minority to expel from province 217").
- **Settlement growth**: Lisbon (Portugal, a console colonist) and Madrid (217, Castile) were allowed and `start_settlement_growth` was executed (`send_colonist={ … settlement_growth_construction=yes expulsion_province=0 }`); the province then read `growing`, and a second start was refused with the game's "一个殖民队正在马德里提高省份发展。 于1446.07.10完成。" (the first build answered no reason there, as it did not ask the game while growing; fixed). Sevilla, from which the minority was being expelled, was refused with "我们正在将塞维利亚的少数族群驱逐到哈瓦那。", Arguin (not Portugal's) with no reason, as the game's check writes none for the panel's own rules, and with no colonist free with "没有可供派遣的殖民队。". `stop_settlement_growth 217` was executed (`cancel_colonist={ … location=217 settlement_growth_construction=yes }`); the province still read `growing` until the game's next day, as a recalled colonist does, so a second stop that day was posted too; then it was free. Stopping in Sevilla, where there was none, was refused by the framework. Lisbon's console colonist went at the next month and the growth with it.
- **Not tried**: the panels on screen (the trade view's policy buttons, the colonize button's expel list and the settlement growth button were not compared with what they show); a unique policy another country follows; a policy chosen again after its cooldown; whether the expel list's provinces are those the window shows, by minority; a colony founded by an expulsion finishing; the modifier `expel_minorities_cost`; an extension calling these capabilities.
- **Cleanup**: the release put the player's files back (every file as the backup had it, the run's autosaves among them) and the game folder's framework files; `dev.toml` was deleted.

## 2026-11-05 What grows outside the census, and the hidden window concealed (tested by me)

`growth` and `leak` with ten rounds each on master (f29ab09), then M6 of [leak-finding.md](leak-finding.md), `memory.uncounted`, on this branch. Each run within a claim, the game started hidden; the saves England-1 and 英格兰1444_11_11, new games as France, 30 days.

- **Ten rounds**: both scenarios passed, `leak` with `watch 10`; the runner renewed its lease at each build, and the run of 18 minutes needed no renewal in between. Over rounds 2 to 10, `growth` grew 40.6 MB of private bytes a round, 23.9 MB of it the game's heap, 11.6 MB free pages in the heaps and 5.1 MB outside them; `leak` 46.8, 22.4, 14.4 and 7.2. On 2026-11-04 the memory account had measured 15.5 MB a round, 7.45 MB of it the game's heap ("The text boxes' animations let go").
- **The census did not see it**: the census's leftovers grew 7.7 MB a round (1,235.8 MB after round 1, 1,305.0 MB after round 10), as before, and the sites that leave the most were those of earlier runs (`0x299db7`, `0x155c294`, `0x156acc9`, `0x299e03`, `0x10b59fe`). About 15 MB a round of the game's heap was blocks no site has.
- **`memory.uncounted`, four rounds of `leak`**: marked after round 2 and compared after round 4. What the census does not count grew 30.1 MB a round, as much as the game's heap (30.6 MB). Of the blocks made since the mark, 16,292 more of 3,088 bytes (with the heap's overhead), 24 MB a round, and 59 more of 204,832 bytes, 5.9 MB. The first ask counted the walk's own buffers too (one block of 137 MB and two of 2.2 MB), which it leaves out since.
- **The blocks of 3,088 bytes**: each holds its own address at `+8`, the same pointer at `+0x10` in every one, and `0x400558c0` at `+0x20`. The object that pointer reaches holds the tags `NVDA` and `NVDX`, which among the NVIDIA modules the game had loaded only `nvd3dumx.dll`, the Direct3D 9 driver, holds (291 and 206 times). They are the graphics driver's.
- **With time, not with sessions**: at the idle main menu, three minutes between two asks, 2,395 more of them (13 a second, 7 MB) with the hidden start's sound off, and 22,649 more (126 a second, 66 MB) in a hidden start with sound, every volume set to 0 in `settings.txt` for it. The game process had NVIDIA's capture module `nvspcap64.dll` loaded, with NVIDIA Overlay and NVIDIA Broadcast running.
- **With NVIDIA's overlay off**: the owner turned it off in the NVIDIA App (the registry's `IsShadowPlayEnabled` then 0, Instant Replay's switch 0 before and after, no NVIDIA Overlay process). `nvspcap64.dll` was still loaded in the game, and at the idle main menu with the sound off the blocks of 3,088 bytes grew by 17,157 in three minutes, 95 a second. With the sound off they grew 13 and 95 a second in two runs, so how fast they grow goes from run to run and not with the sound.
- **Shown, they stop**: in one process at the idle main menu, two minutes each, with the owner's leave to show the window: hidden 14,948 more (125 a second), shown with `desktop.show` none, hidden again with `desktop.hide` 2,439 more (20 a second). An experiment build whose `desktop.show` cloaked the window (`DWMWA_CLOAK`) and showed it: hidden 5,476 more, shown and cloaked none, hidden again 3,785 more; the window then counted as visible and cloaked, and the foreground stayed the other program's.
- **The window concealed**: the hidden start now shows the window where Init would, cloaked, as a tool window and without the focus, and `desktop.hide` conceals it so again. Looked at from outside the game: visible, cloaked, no point of three on it hitting it (`WindowFromPoint`), not the foreground, and no taskbar button (UI Automation over the taskbar's elements). Cloaked alone, it had a taskbar button, "Europa Universalis IV - 1 个运行窗口"; with the tool window's style, none. After `desktop.show`: not cloaked, every point hitting it, a taskbar button, still not the foreground; after `desktop.hide` as at the start. 86 more of the driver's blocks in the first two minutes at the main menu, none in two minutes after `desktop.hide`. `screenshot` gave the main menu as drawn.
- **Minimize-all and show-desktop**: with the window concealed at the main menu, the shell's `MinimizeAll` (as Win+M) and `ToggleDesktop` (as Win+D), each undone at once, through `Shell.Application`: the window stayed not minimized, visible and cloaked, never the foreground, and `desktop.get` was answered in 33 to 36 ms throughout, as before them. A minimized game runs at about ten frames a second ([research/headless.md](research/headless.md)); these leave it alone.
- **Ten rounds of `growth` with it**: passed, each round 26 to 27 s (31 to 45 s before). Over rounds 2 to 10: 16.4 MB of private bytes a round, 7.44 MB of it the game's heap, 7.4 MB free pages in the heaps, 1.5 MB outside them; handles 1.7 a round. As on 2026-11-04 (15.5 and 7.45).
- **What it says**: from the hidden start on (2026-11-04), the rounds measured, besides the game's leaks, the graphics driver's blocks of a window not shown, which grow with time; with the window shown cloaked, they measure the game's leaks again, which have not grown. The leaks the census counts have not grown. Not known: what makes the driver keep them, whether `nvspcap64.dll` plays a part, and whether other drivers do the same. A player's game, whose window is shown, does not have them.
- **Not tried**: the game without `nvspcap64.dll`, the call stacks of the driver's allocations, a computer with another graphics card, Alt+Tab looked at for the concealed window (a tool window is left out of it), Win+M and Win+D pressed on the keyboard rather than asked of the shell, `leak` with the window concealed.
- **Cleanup**: every claim released with every file put back ("every file is as the backup has it"), `settings.txt` with the volumes among them. The runs' reports and the scratch scripts were deleted.

## 2026-11-05 The startup and a load sampled, and made shorter (tested by me)

Two claims (`cargo xtask game`, the game started hidden), the player's playset, every DLC. Other sessions' builds held the machine's processor at 67 to 94% throughout, so every time is long and none is of a quiet machine.

- **Sampled** (the first claim, the player build from master): `cargo xtask profile --wait --slice 5` from the process's start, then `--thread busiest` and `--thread 0x874010` for the LoadingThread; where the time went is in research/loading.md. The main menu came 46 to 55 s after the start. A first run with `--thread` came back with empty stacks: `GetThreadContext` had a context that was not 16-byte aligned; the profiler now aligns it.
- **`check-loading`** (a developer build with the test switches, this branch's L1 to L3): the first start kept the numbers of 270 music files in `eu4-harmony\music.txt`; the second filled them from there and had the game read every file too: no difference was logged for any of them, and none for the lookups the listings answered missing, which the game's stat answered missing as well.
- **The startup**, one developer build, `game-loads` (the game's own startup) and the framework's in turn: the game's 82 and 82 s to the main menu, the framework's 68 and 71 s. Each of the framework's starts answered more than 131,072 lookups from the listings.
- **A load**, Austria-37_4-1 (1724, ironman, 55 MB) from the main menu, traced (`0x5cf740`, the save read and the world set up, and `0xd84b80`, the load with what follows it): the game's 6.2 and 7.3 s, and 8.1 and 8.7 s; the framework's 5.1 and 4.7 s, and 6.7 and 6.2 s. The loads that followed, from the game it had loaded, went 5.4 to 9.1 s either way, in an order that did not follow the switch.
- **The same game loaded**: the save the game writes as an ironman game is loaded, written once after the game's own load and once after the framework's: `gamestate` (55,051,381 bytes) differed in the 4 bytes at 510 that every save changes, `meta` in its UUID and the checksum of it, `ai` not at all.
- **What the music's numbers are for** (a third claim, a developer build made for it, not kept, that set the processor's debug registers on the sound of `music/maintheme.ogg` for reads and writes on every thread; the main menu for 45 s): the duration was read once, by the songs' list as the databases loaded (`0x1fdf32`, in minutes and seconds for the music player); the song's play opened it again and wrote the four numbers anew, and then the audio thread read the channels and rate and the sound its length in samples about once a second; nothing else read them (research/loading.md).
- **The music told by its zip entry** (a fourth claim, 2026-11-06): with each kept file told by its zip entry's CRC-32 and sizes (246 of the 270) or, in a folder, by its size and time (24), two starts with `check-loading` logged no difference and no zip whose directory could not be read; then the framework's start reached the main menu in 58 s, the game's (`game-loads`) in 73 s, the processor 76% busy with other work.
- **Merged with master's music mixing** (a fifth claim, 2026-11-06, the player build): the start with `music.txt` empty took 49 s and the next, filling the numbers from it, 41 s; the main theme played through the framework's mixer; Austria-37_4-1 loaded; no error in the log.
- **Not verified**: a start with nothing in the system's file cache; a mod in the workshop changed while the game runs; a playset whose mods have music; the game's own exit and a restart reading `music.txt` written by a start that ended early; how long the dated nodes' lists get.
- **Cleanup**: the game closed with `cargo xtask game stop`; releasing each claim put back every player file (`music.txt` removed with them) and the game folder's framework files, and found each as the backup had it. The repository's `dev.toml` is as it was.

## 2026-11-05 The volume curve and the music mixed by the framework (tested by me)

[music-volume.md](music-volume.md), V1 to V3, a developer build with the call trace on `SDL_MixAudioFormat`'s body (`0x17458b0`), which now sees every voice but the music. Hidden starts, without sound: SDL's `dummy` driver runs the mixer, so the music was mixed and measured but not heard. Four starts within one claim.

- **The first start**, the player's volumes (master 12, music 67.32): the startup report said all points matched; the main theme was "heard for the first time" and measured as it played: -35.6 LUFS over its first 20 s and -28.4 over 40 s, where ffmpeg gives the file's first 20 and 40 s -35.5 and -28.4. The cache was written every 20 s; at the end it held the main theme at -11.42 over 240 s, ffmpeg's -11.4 for the same 240 s. The game's gain for it while loading was 0.0808, vanilla's: before the main theme first plays, the game gives the music master × music on its own (`0x202296`), which the first build missed. A third jump point there was added.
- **The second start**, same volumes: the main theme "is known, -11.4 LUFS" from its first second. The game's gain was 0.0039 while loading, the curve's 0.00386 for master 12 and music 67.32, and 0.00289 at the main menu, that times the menu's 0.75, as in vanilla.
- **The third start**, master 100, music 50, and a new game as France at speed 1: the gain was 0.1 while loading (the curve's -20 dB at 50), 0.075 at the main menu and 0.0756 in the game. After the main theme, the game played two tracks the cache did not know; the first was measured from -27.2 LUFS over 20 s, a quiet opening, to -12.9 over 220 s, then the next began. Sound effects and ambient sound went to SDL as before, at SDL volumes from 1 to 5 for the map's ambient sound and 33 for a sound at the main menu, which fits the effects curve (77 is 0.347, 44 of 128) times a voice volume of about 0.75; no vanilla run with the same volumes was made to compare.
- **The fourth start**, music leveling turned on in the framework settings: the log showed it on, and the music went on being mixed.
- **Not verified**: anything heard, since no run played sound: whether normalization, leveling and the limiter sound right, and whether the volume curve feels even; the settings window's Sound page, which was not opened (its toggles were set in the file); the ambient volume's point, whose gains were not compared; a whole track heard for the first time and normalized as it played, which the log shows only as measured; mods' and DLC music beyond the tracks the game picked.
- **Since**: the volume curve was changed afterward from even in decibels to the square of the position, which changes only `sound::gain`; the gains above are the first curve's. The square's are in the run below.
- **Cleanup**: releasing the game put back the player's files, among them `settings.txt`, the framework's settings and the autosaves the new game wrote, and removed `music-loudness.txt`; `dev.toml` was deleted, and the copied logs were in the session's scratch folder, deleted after.

## 2026-11-05 The square volume curve, and what a short hearing keeps (tested by me)

[music-volume.md](music-volume.md) after the volume curve became the square of the position, a developer build with the call trace on `SDL_MixAudioFormat`'s body (`0x17458b0`), every voice but the music. Hidden starts without sound, five within one claim.

- **The curve**: master 12 and music 67.32 gave the music a gain of 0.0065 while loading, the square's 0.00653; master 100 and music 50 gave 0.25, and 0.1887 in a new game as France, that times the game's own voice volume of 0.755. The first sound at the main menu went to SDL at 57 with effects at 77 (0.77² × 128 × 0.75 is 57; the first curve gave 33).
- **What it found**: the second start recognized the main theme at -35.6 LUFS, which the first start had kept from only 20 s of its quiet opening, and raised it by the most, 12 dB, for all of its 260 s, while that hearing measured it at -11.0. Three changes followed: a hearing is kept only once it is a minute long; a first hearing is raised only once a minute is measured (it is lowered from 3 s, as before); and a track that plays longer than what was kept of it follows what this hearing measures.
- **After the changes**: with the cache removed, the main theme played its first minute; nothing was kept at 20 or 40 s, and at 60 s it was kept at -13.7 LUFS, ffmpeg's figure for the file's first minute. At the next start it was "known, -13.7 LUFS", and at 80 s the longer hearing replaced it, -15.1 over 80 s.
- **Not verified**: anything heard; how a first hearing sounds as it is raised after its first minute; whether the limiter is heard on a track a short entry raised, now that one cannot be kept.
- **Cleanup**: releasing the game put back the player's files, among them `settings.txt` and the autosaves the new game wrote, and removed `music-loudness.txt`; `dev.toml` was deleted.

## 2026-11-05 Each track analyzed from its whole file as it starts (tested by me)

The owner asked for each track's loudness to come from its whole file before it plays, in place of measuring as it plays and keeping that ([music-volume.md](music-volume.md)). A first developer build logged what a streaming voice's sound holds: the object at `[voice+8]` is the one the voice's method at vtable `+8` returns (vtable `0x1dac778`, method `0x9a170`), with the sound's name at `+0x10` ("maintheme"), its file's path in the game's files at `+0x50` ("music/maintheme.ogg"), its length in frames at `+0xd8` (11960945, the file's 271.2 s) and 44100 and 2 channels at `+0xe8`. Then the player build of the change, hidden without sound, the player's volumes, the main menu and a new game as France.

- **The main theme**: "music/maintheme.ogg plays; the game's gain 0.0065", then "is -10.9 LUFS, analyzed in 0.56 s", ffmpeg's -10.9 for the file; the offline check decodes the same file in under half a second.
- **DLC music**: in the game, `music/sight_of_the_black_sea.ogg` (in `dlc132.zip`) was -8.9 LUFS, analyzed in 0.49 s, and `music/austria_theme.ogg` (in `dlc140.zip`) -13.2, in 0.32 s; ffmpeg gives the files taken from those archives -8.9 and -13.2. Both were read through the game's file system, as the game reads them.
- **Not verified**: anything heard; a mod's music; a file that cannot be decoded, which plays as it is (a unit test refuses what is not Ogg Vorbis); a track started again within the same game, which takes its measure from the first.
- **Cleanup**: releasing the game put back the player's files, among them the autosaves the new game wrote, and the game folder's framework files; the files taken from the DLC archives were deleted.

## 2026-11-05 A track held until its file is analyzed (tested by me)

The owner asked for a track to wait for its analysis rather than play as it is until then. The player build of the change, hidden without sound, the player's volumes and playset, the main menu and a new game as France; four starts within one claim.

- **Held**: the main theme "plays", was "-10.9 LUFS, analyzed in 0.58 s", and then "held 441 ms for its analysis" before its first frame was mixed; the next starts held it 557 ms for an analysis of 0.72 s. The hold is counted in mixer calls, which SDL's `dummy` driver makes somewhat slower than real time. In the games, `music/037_theageofdiscovery.ogg` and `music/031_kingscourt.ogg` (files of the playset's mods) and `music/march_on_granada.ogg` (a DLC's) were each held 371 to 557 ms and analyzed in 0.49 to 0.74 s.
- **The stream goes on as it was**: a temporary log of the seconds each voice mixed gave the main theme 270.1 s, its whole 271.2 s once, with the hold at its start.
- **A gap the game makes**: with this playset, the next track came about 6 min after the main theme began, some 90 s after it ended, where earlier runs gave about 4 min 34 s. A start with loudness normalization off, which holds nothing, gave the same 6 min 2 s, so the gap is the game's, not the hold's; why the game waits longer with this playset was not looked into.
- **Not verified**: anything heard; a hold that runs out at 2 s, or an analysis that fails, which play as they are (by reading the code only).
- **Cleanup**: releasing the game put back the player's files, among them the framework's settings with the line the last start added and the autosaves the games wrote, and the game folder's framework files; the temporary log was taken out of the code.

## 2026-11-06 Leaving a session as a whole, and the catalogue's one seam (tested by me)

After three changes that should change nothing a player sees: leaving a session takes its steps in one place over what the game does (`leave::Game`), the world reset with the history puts right what the history leaves wherever it is told, and remote control's engine and the Lua host reach the catalogue through one seam (`catalogue::Catalogue`). The memory account build, hidden without sound, the player's playset with the Regional Combat extension; one claim.

- **The growth scenario**, 2 rounds of 10 days: each round loaded `England-1`, loaded `英格兰1444_11_11` in that game, returned to the main menu, started a new game as France and returned again. It passed; round 2 grew 110.1 MB, as short rounds have before.

## 2026-11-06 A subject relation's end, with and without its repair (tested by me)

The repair of the walk of a subject's supporters as its relation ends (`core/src/repairs/supporters.rs`; research/dependency-teardown.md), after a player's crash in an observer game in 1478. Within one claim, the game started hidden, a developer build with the test switch `game-ends-dependencies`, the heap census's quarantine turned on by `memory.quarantine` before each load, and a copy of that game's 1478.1.1 autosave with `player="NOG"` and `not_observer=yes`, loaded with `menu.load`. The same in two runs. The fault's address is not logged by the console's guard; that it was at the walk's next node (`0xa61980`) is inferred from the log lines around it.

- **Not tried**: the walk without the quarantine, where the game crashes only when the heap gives the freed node again before the walk reads it; a subject with more than one country in its list; the other callers of `0xa61900` and the peace that crashed the player's game; a game of more than 60 days with the repair.

## 2026-11-03 What the player sees during a return and an in-game load (tested by me)

The player had seen the game's window flicker a few times and the loading screen of the game's start during an in-process return from an ironman game, and a flicker now and then during an in-game load. One run of the installed build (master at `9b013d5`), started directly, driven by `eu4-harmony game`, the game borderless at 3840×2160 with vsync and 8× multisampling: the ironman save `H-Test` (Hungary, 1444.11.11) loaded from the main menu, `menu.return`, the save loaded again, `menu.load` from the session, loaded once more and `menu.return`. The screen was recorded at 60 frames a second with ffmpeg (`ddagrab`); the last return was watched with `IsHungAppWindow` and the foreground window every 50 ms. The game was claimed with the game-lock skill.

- **The flicker is Windows' "not responding" window**: the in-process return ran 5.8 s and 5.3 s on the main thread, and the in-game load 10.0 s, without the game taking its window's messages. At the last return, 4.1 s after `menu.return`, `IsHungAppWindow` turned true and a window of class `Ghost`, "Europa Universalis IV (未响应)", took the foreground in the game's place; 1.2 s later, once the main menu took over, the game's own window was back. In the recordings the taskbar came up over the game at each change: in the first return 4.9 s after the still notice, for 1.5 s, until the main menu's first frames; in the in-game load for a frame, as the load ended. The still notice itself was drawn over the frame the game had last presented, as meant.
- **The loading screen is the main menu's**: 0.4 s after the switch to the new main menu, the window showed the loading screen's marks and tip over black; the map behind the main menu faded in under them, and the main menu's title came 2 s later, as at startup. The painting the player saw is presented before that, once, as the main menu takes over (docs/research/main-menu.md, "The application's idler switch", found in the code afterwards and traced on 2026-11-06); the recording has no frame of it, which fits the ghost window showing the game's last frame over its presents at that time, but was not checked.
- **Not verified**: the player's own ways out (the return question, the load button), which reach the same steps; a window in exclusive full screen; how long a load runs before Windows calls the game hung on other machines (5 s by default).
- **Cleanup**: the game was closed by ending its process. Releasing the game restored the player's files, the `H-Test` saves rewritten by the ironman saves among them, and found every file as the backup had it. The recordings and scripts were in the session's scratch folder.

## 2026-11-06 Launches, sessions, settings and capabilities each through one module (tested by me)

The branch that takes a load or start through `launch::Launches`, numbers and forgets sessions in `sessions`, declares the framework settings in one table, opens the session a capability reads once, and takes the claim's course over the computer through `claim::Machine`. The game was claimed with this branch's own `cargo xtask game claim --wait`, which queued behind two other sessions and granted it; `cargo xtask scenario` played in the claim. First `growth` and `perf` at `09804f2`; then, after master's `5d25fd0` was merged, `growth` again at `eb2d5f0`.

- **growth**, 10 rounds of the saves `England-1` (ironman, loaded from the main menu, then in-game) and `英格兰1444_11_11`, returns and new games as France: passed both times, 26 to 30 s a round. Private bytes grew 13.8 MB a round over rounds 2 to 10 at `09804f2`, the game's heap 5.8 MB of it; the framework's heap stayed at 6.8 to 6.9 MB. In the second run the sessions were numbered up to 59, each in-game load, return and new game logged its session's end and the next one's start, 116 remote requests answered with none failed, and each of the 20 returns logged "the main menu took over, 1 of its presents held back" (master's change).
- **perf**, the player build, a new game as Ming at speed 5: passed; the main menu after 29.9 s, 1,179 game days a minute at speed 5, 3.15 cores at speed 5 and 0.84 paused.
- **The settings table**: the framework settings were read at startup as the player had saved them (the language and fonts chosen, the music settings, the fastest speed), and the player language took effect.
- **Found**: each new game started from the main menu logged "a new game as FRA failed: the game did not start it: the main menu is open" at the main menu after its return, though it had started (also in a run of master by another session). The start was still followed after the game took it over, and the next main menu's frame gave it up. Fixed afterwards in `e642996`, which lets it go at the game's first frame. In a second claim, `growth` for 3 rounds at `6562999` (the branch as merged): each new game as France started, its session began and ended for the return, and no load or start logged a failure; 40 remote requests answered, none failed. The rounds took 34 to 38 s, against 26 to 30 s in the first run, and the game 38 s to its main menu against 31 s: the whole run was slower, which was not looked into.
- **The claim**: the queue, the grant, the renewal at each scenario's start and the release, which ended the game, found every one of the player's files as the backup had it and the game folder's framework files as they were.
- **Not verified**: the settings window and saving a setting; an ironman exit save that fails; the claim taking over from a holder whose lease ran out, and the player's claim; the STATE province panel's acts through the capability.
- **Cleanup**: releasing the game put back the player's files, among them the saves the loads rewrote and the backup save one of them added; the reports were in the session's scratch folder.

## 2026-11-06 Time while the player observes (tested by me)

The time group declared `world`, run at a game's frame the player cannot play (`Place::Unplayable`), the game layer's time on the world without the player's country (`game::world_context`). The player build of this branch, the player's playset, the game hidden; a new game as Castile, then `console run observe` ("Entered Spectator Mode.").

- **The first build** refused `time.resume` and `time.advance` there, "the game does not let an observer run time": it read the pause key's other check as "no country and `world+0x74dc` clear", and `observe` leaves that byte clear. Pressed as the player would (`input key space`), the pause key resumed the same game: 1444.11.11 paused, then 1444.12.1 running 2 s later. The key reads the tag's last byte (`world+0x1e67`), which `observe` leaves set, not whether the tag is a country's; `time.resume` now reads the same two bytes.
- **The second build**, observing: `time.get` 1444.11.11 paused at speed 1; `time.set_speed 5`; `time.resume`, then 1444.11.12 running; `time.pause`, then 1444.11.14 paused; `time.advance 10` answered `date` at 1444.11.24 0.7 s later, with no events; then a year at a time with `time.advance 365`, each answered at its date. `session.get` and `army.list` answered `not_in_game` ("no controllable player"), as before.
- **Not tried**: an advance under way as the player's country is annexed; a player with no country whose tag's last byte is clear (the pause key's refusal, which `time.resume` now gives); extensions, which run no frame while the player observes.

## 2026-11-07 Each AI country's task timed, 1700 to 1730 (tested by me)

Whether a late game's AI stage waits for its longest task or for the order of its tasks, the question the run of 2026-11-06 left ([research/late-game.md](research/late-game.md), "Each AI country's task, timed"). Master at `05022e8` with the call trace's new `times`, built with `dev-call-trace` alone; the player's playset and settings; one claim of 18 minutes, the game started hidden.

- **First run**: `dev.toml` traced `0x77d8a0` and `0xb0fa0` with `times`, the field `0x20` of `0xb0fa0`'s argument, `calls = 0`. The kept save of 1700.1.1 copied into the save games folder and loaded with `menu.load`, `console run observe`, the Ottomans viewed by Ctrl and a click on Anatolia (the top bar and outliner became theirs). `time.advance 365` at speed 5 to 1725.1.1: 9,125 days in 524 s (17.4 a second), no question waited. `call-times.tsv` had one stage a day and 131 to 145 tasks a stage, each with a three-letter tag in its field.
- **Second run**: the game restarted with `0x786150`, `0x14b370`, `0xe4890`, `0xea250`, `0xeab50`, `0x14acc0` and `0x13d4c0` traced too, the run's autosave of 1721.1.1 loaded, viewed as the Ottomans again, to 1730.1.1: 3,285 days in 196 s (16.8 a second), about 25,000 timed calls a second. The first start of it ran the player build: `cargo xtask disasm`, run in between, had built the core again without the feature, so `call-times.tsv` stayed the first run's and the log had no `trace:` lines; built again and installed, the second start was the developer build.
- **Found**: a stage lasted as long as its longest task (the median 1.00 times), which started first; inside the tasks, the time was in `0xe4890`'s items, and on the worst days in one of them.
- **Not verified**: the cost of the timing itself, beyond the days a second being near those of the profiled run of the same years (15.0 under the profiler); the order of a stage's tasks read from the game's code, rather than seen in the times; a game a player plays.
- **Cleanup**: `dev.toml` at the top of the checkout deleted; releasing the game put back the player's files and removed the two saves the runs added; `call-times.tsv` of both runs, the screenshots, the 1701 and 1721 autosaves and the scripts stayed in the session's scratch folder.

## 2026-11-07 The sampler's own stack walk, and every thread sampled (tested by me)

Whether `cargo xtask profile` walks stacks as dbghelp does once it copies a thread's stack and walks it afterwards with the modules' unwind data, what a sample then costs, and whether `--threads` gives every thread's CPU. Master at `5bc2dbd` with this change, the player build; the run's autosave of 1721.1.1 (the AI-only game of 2026-11-06), observed in the Ottomans' view, `time.advance` a year at a time at speed 5; one claim, the game started hidden.

- **Against dbghelp**: a build of the sampler that, for every sample, walked the suspended thread with dbghelp's `StackWalk64` too and compared the two, 30 s with `--threads` at 50 a second. The first walk differed in 27 of 48,239 stacks, all at the innermost frame: a thread stopped on a `jmp` inside a function was taken for one leaving it, and version 2 unwind data's epilog codes (`UWOP_EPILOG`, in ntdll and the CRT's `0x1a35de0`) ended the walk. With both mended, 48,089 of 48,093 stacks were the same; the 4 others ended in the framework's hook stubs, which have no unwind data and after which dbghelp's walk went on through addresses in no module.
- **What a sample holds its thread**: the main thread 0.099 ms a sample alone, 0.5% of the time at 50 a second (dbghelp's walk held it 1.15 ms on the same save the day before, run 9); with `--threads`, the main thread 0.09 to 0.16 ms and the others 0.02 ms each, about 33 threads at a tick, at 43 to 48 ticks a second.
- **The game's speed**: 30 s at a time, in turn, with no sampling, the main thread sampled and every thread sampled, twice: 11.93, 11.09 and 11.82 days a second, then 13.81, 12.24 and 15.31. The game's own changes from one stretch to the next were larger than any cost of sampling, which these runs therefore do not measure.
- **Every thread's CPU**: weighing each sample by the cycles its thread had run since the last put 1.36 of 2.84 cores in `ZwWaitForAlertByThreadId`: workers run in bursts and are found asleep. Counting a thread only when its instruction pointer is not right after a `syscall` gave the whole game 2.15 cores, against 2.76 that Windows counted for its threads (kernel time included), and the AI countries' updates (`0xb0fa0`) 0.95 of them.
- **Not verified**: the walk on other games or machines; frames past the framework's hook stubs.
- **Cleanup**: the comparison was taken out before the change was committed; releasing the game put back the player's files and removed the save the run added.

## 2026-11-07 Inside an AI country's task, 1721 onwards (tested by me)

What an AI country's task spends its time on, what an army agent's think writes, and whether it could be made faster ([research/late-game.md](research/late-game.md), "An AI country's agents" to "Whether it can be made faster"). Master at `05022e8` and later at `f6bb9a3`; the run's autosave of 1721.1.1 (the AI-only game of 2026-11-06) loaded in each start, observed in the Ottomans' view, `time.advance` at speed 5; four claims, the game started hidden.

- **Write watch** (`dev-write-watch` alone, `[write-watch] function = "0xea250"`, `calls = 3`): the first three army thinks on the main thread, on 1721.1.2 to 1721.1.6, each with 100 other threads held and 8.2 million heap blocks made read-only in about 0.5 s; 643, 148 and 2,779 writes. The game did not hang.
- **Sampled, the old way**: `cargo xtask profile --thread 0xea250 --hz 200` for 240 s picked the main thread 73% of the seconds, so few samples were a worker's.
- **Timed calls, too many**: two runs of four and three minutes with the call trace's `times` on the think and functions inside it. Tracing `0x96bd00` (86 million calls) and then `0x96f860` and `0x102140` (138 and 50 million) made the thinks that call them take several times as long and wrote 5.4 and 11 GB; their lengths were not used, only their counts and the shares of their paths. A first start traced `0xf8ea0`, which reads 18 arguments while an observer forwards 12; it was stopped before a game was loaded, and the call trace now leaves such a function out.
- **Sampled, every thread**: `cargo xtask profile --threads --slice 1` (the sampler of the section above), 240 s, 3,702 days, 15.4 a second; the whole game 2.17 cores.
- **Route queries logged**: the call trace logging `0x96c520`'s and the stage's first 300,000 calls, `time.advance 30` twelve times, 1721.1.1 to 1721.12.27: 18,666 queries over 360 stages.
- **Not verified**: a top agent's plan watched or timed on its own; the write watch on a long think; another game or a player's.
- **Cleanup**: `dev.toml` deleted after each run; the timing files deleted from the scratch folder and from the claims' kept logs (5.4 and 11 GB, and the first day's 420 MB); releasing the game put back the player's files and removed the save each run added.

## How the implementation differs from the final design

- Binding the notice window again after its host window is rebuilt (for example by `reload`) is not tested yet; the top bar binds, but how notices look in the top bar has not been confirmed on its own.
- The button area is a third column to the right of the two vanilla button columns (x=464, on the vanilla 43×44 grid), with no background frame.
- A text box is "hidden" by clearing its text, not by calling the text box's show/hide method (which is not verified on this build).
- An extension's route queries, moves and stops are all refused while the game is paused (`NOT_READY`), as in the old regional-combat; remote control's are the player's, and go while paused, as the player's do (Z2).
- Map coloring borrows native map mode 12; provinces that are not colored show that mode's own colors.
- When the user directory cannot be found, logs fall back to `eu4-harmony\logs\` in the game directory.
